66	
67	Three descriptor files carry YAML frontmatter so the framework can machine-read them:
68	
69	- **`lore-repo.md`** — at the root of a lore agent repo. Frontmatter fields:
70	  - `description` (string, required)
71	  - `version` (string, required) — matches the framework `VERSION` when the repo was created or last migrated. The repo version is the single source of truth for migration state; `/lr:update` uses it to decide what migrations to apply.
72	  - `repos` (block-form list of strings, optional) — **domain-level** declaration: remote URLs of sibling repos the agents in this domain need. Consumed by `/lr:workspace-pull`. See `docs/workspace-pull.md`.
73	- **`lore-workspace.md`** — optional, at the workspace root (not inside an agent repo). Frontmatter fields:
74	  - `description` (string) — human label for the workspace.
75	  - `repos` (block-form list of strings, optional) — **workspace-level** declaration: remote URLs of the top-level repos that belong in this workspace, including the lore **agent repos themselves**. Consumed by `/lr:workspace-pull` (phase 1). The markdown body is user-owned onboarding prose. Written by `/lr:workspace-init`.
76	- **`role.md`** — at the root of each agent directory. Frontmatter fields: `description` (string) only. Agents do not carry their own version stamp at framework version 2+; they migrate together with the repo.
77	
78	**The dual meaning of `repos:`.** The same YAML key names repos at two scopes. In `lore-workspace.md`
79	it is the *workspace layout* — which top-level repos (agent repos + shared repos) should exist as
80	siblings. In a `lore-repo.md` it is that *agent domain's dependencies* — the sibling repos those
81	agents need. `/lr:workspace-pull` reads the workspace level first (so agent repos are on disk), then
82	the domain level, and clones the union. Both use identical block-form list syntax.
83	
84	## Lore Topics
85	
86	- **Atomic** — one concept or lesson per file
87	- **Compact** — under 5K tokens preferred, flexible when needed
88	- **Plain markdown** — no frontmatter, no metadata fields. Descriptor files (`lore-repo.md`, `role.md`) are the only exception — see above.
89	- **Git tracks metadata** — creation date, update history, authorship all come from git
90	- **Obsolete topics are deleted** — git preserves history, no need for status markers
91	- **Cross-references by filename** — topics reference each other to form a knowledge graph
92	- **Summary topics** — serve as entry points to clusters of related topics
93	
94	## Lore Context
95	
96	`lore-context.md` is the agent's every-session working knowledge **and** the entry point to the lore graph — not an index of all topics. The graph (summary topics → detail topics) carries whatever isn't needed in most sessions.
97	
98	- **Content**: compacted, present-tense working knowledge + references to the most important / **summary** topics — not enumerations of detail topics
99	- **Entry point, not catalog**: reference a theme's summary topic and let it fan out; if a significant theme lacks one, create it rather than inlining the cluster
100	- **No version-history narrative**: present-tense only — "vN did X" annotations belong in git / release notes / the topic itself
101	- **Budget**: 50K tokens maximum — but shape is the primary discipline; nearing the budget is a drift signal to restructure, not just to summarize older entries
102	- **Prioritization**: knowledge relevant across most sessions first; one-off knowledge lives in a topic reachable via a referenced summary topic
103	
104	See `process-merge.md` § Step 4 for the procedure that maintains this shape at finalization.
105	
106	## What Belongs in Lore
107	
108	- Decisions and their reasoning
109	- Domain knowledge and discoveries
110	- Operational recommendations from experience
111	- Practical context about systems and environment
112	- Approaches that worked or failed, and why
113	
114	## What Does NOT Belong in Lore
115	
116	- General knowledge anyone could look up
117	- Information obvious from reading the code
118	- Temporary session state
119	- Verbatim conversation excerpts
120	
121	## Tooling: CWD Safety
122	
123	The agent's shell working directory is shared across Bash, Glob, Grep, and subsequent git calls. A `cd` in one command silently shifts the root for every tool invocation that follows — the agent will not notice until a path resolves wrong. Symptoms: Glob returning "No files found" for files that clearly exist, or a repo-scoped git command pointed at the wrong repo.
124	
125	- **Prefer `git -C <lore-agent-repo> <cmd>`** over `cd <lore-agent-repo> && <cmd>` whenever a command is scoped to a particular repo. All framework docs follow this convention.
126	- **Never `cd` away from the workspace root** during a flow that iterates over repos (update, check, version-check). If you must, `cd` back before the next tool call.
127	- **Pass absolute paths to Glob** via the `path` parameter when you're unsure of the current CWD, rather than relying on pattern-relative resolution.
128	
129	## Tooling: Portable Shell
130	
131	Framework-authored shell commands run in whatever environment the agent booted in — and the primary dev platform is macOS (BSD userland), not GNU/Linux. Commands that silently assume GNU coreutils break there, and the failure is often invisible: a `command not found` (exit 127) inside a best-effort flow reads as the underlying operation failing, not as a portability bug.
132	
133	- **No GNU-only binaries.** Most notably `timeout` / `gtimeout` — absent on a stock macOS. To bound a command's runtime, use your Bash tool's own timeout parameter, not a `timeout` wrapper. (This exact trap silently disabled v13 auto-pull on macOS; see `auto-pull.md` § Step 2.) Inside a **standalone script** there is no Bash-tool timeout and no portable one-liner for a hard wall-clock cap — prevent hangs at the source with fail-fast env vars (`GIT_TERMINAL_PROMPT=0`, `GIT_SSH_COMMAND` with `BatchMode`/`ConnectTimeout`), or implement a bash watchdog (background the job, then `sleep`+`kill`). `scripts/workspace-pull` takes the fail-fast-env-vars route.
134	- **Watch BSD-vs-GNU flag differences** on tools present in both userlands: `sed -i` (BSD needs `-i ''`), `date -d` (GNU-only), `readlink -f` / `realpath` (not always present), `grep -P`, `xargs -r`. Prefer a portable invocation or a short Bash/`git` equivalent.
135	- **Prefer git's own knobs over external wrappers** for git operations: `GIT_TERMINAL_PROMPT=0` (suppress HTTP(S) auth prompts), `GIT_SSH_COMMAND='ssh -o BatchMode=yes -o ConnectTimeout=<n>'` (fail-fast SSH), `git -C <repo>` (see § Tooling: CWD Safety above).
136	
137	Same shape as CWD Safety: a portability landmine hidden inside an emitted shell command. When authoring or reviewing any framework doc or script that emits shell, assume BSD/macOS and check every command against this list.
138	
139	## Tooling: Non-Shell Runtimes (python3)
140	
141	Directly-runnable shipped scripts default to Bash-on-BSD (§ Tooling: Portable Shell). The exception is a **server component that must speak a protocol** where bash is impractical — most notably an **MCP stdio server** (newline-delimited JSON-RPC). Those may be written in **`python3`, standard library only** (no `pip install`), so they need no dependency install and run anywhere `python3` is present — any Linux, and any macOS with the Xcode Command Line Tools (a bare macOS may prompt to install them). `scripts/wait-server.py` is the first instance; it parallels the Node ULA module (`df/aiqa/workflows/ula-file-pass.js`) as a language-specific module the framework ships and drives, not a portable one-liner. Keep companion tooling in Bash where practical (e.g. `scripts/lr-emit`). Don't reach for `python3` for work a portable shell script can do.
142	
143	The second sanctioned class is a **deterministic substrate** with real data structures, structured output, and per-platform edge cases that bash handles badly: `scripts/lrb.py` (the Being Keeper) and `scripts/lr-core` (§ Script Fallback Contract below). Same rules — stdlib only, no `pip`, one file, Python 3.9 floor.
144	
145	## Script Fallback Contract
146	
147	A shipped script is one of two things, and which one decides what happens when it breaks.
148	
149	- **Accelerator** — the procedure is also specified in a `docs/` doc, and that doc, not the script, is normative. The script exists to spend one tool call where prose would spend six, and to encode platform traps once. `scripts/lr-core` is the reference case.
150	- **Implementation** — the script *is* the specification; no prose procedure exists to execute by hand. `scripts/workspace-pull`, `scripts/session-takeover`, `scripts/sync-cursor-skills`, `scripts/lr-emit` + `scripts/wait-server.py`, and `scripts/lrb.py` are all this kind.
151	
152	**Take over manually only for an accelerator.** When a doc delegates a step to an accelerator script and the script **fails to complete** — a non-zero exit other than a documented result code, a missing interpreter, no output, or output you cannot parse:
153	
154	1. **Notify the user immediately**, in one line: which script failed and that you are proceeding manually. Never fail silently, and never let a script failure look like the underlying operation failing.
155	2. **Take over and execute the canonical prose procedure** the doc points to, resolving what you reasonably can along the way (a missing directory, a repo that needs a different path). The flow must reach the same end state it would have reached had the script worked.
156	3. **Diagnose briefly, don't stall.** If the cause is obvious (no `python3`, non-executable file, wrong path), say so in one line. Otherwise finish the flow and report the failure at the end for follow-up. **Never abort the surrounding flow because a script died.**
157	
158	**Distinguish failure from a reported degraded condition.** A script that exits 0 while reporting a problem in its output (`"pull": {"status": "failed"}`, `"teammate": {"verdict": "unknown"}`) has *succeeded* — it did its job and told you the truth about the world. That is data: handle it per the procedure doc's degraded-mode guidance. No takeover, no user-facing script-failure notice.
159	
160	Accelerator scripts state their exit-code contract in their own header. `scripts/lr-core` is the reference implementation: exit 0 = ran to completion (`ok: false` still means "ran fine, request could not be satisfied" — act on `errors`); exit 2 = could not complete, fall back.
161	
162	**Never improvise a substitute for an implementation script.** There is no prose procedure to fall back to, and hand-rolling one produces a partial, undocumented imitation whose divergence nobody can review. When one of these fails: report it to the user with the exact command and the error, stop *that operation*, and continue the surrounding session. Never report the operation as done.
163	
164	`scripts/lrb.py` (the Being Keeper) is the strictest case of that rule. The Keeper is substrate that must never be impersonated by a model — it enforces budget caps and kill-switches, and an LLM standing in for it would be exactly the prompt-theater the Lore Beings design forbids. Its failure mode is "Keeper down, beings do not run".
165	
166	Docs that delegate to a script carry **one line** pointing here, whichever kind it is — a pointer, not a restatement, so this contract has a single home.
167	
168	## Migration / Release-Note Authoring
169	
170	Every `migrations/<N>.md` and `release-notes/<N>.md` whose changes touch **plugin-cached state** must include a **Clear Plugin Cache** section near the top — right after the Summary, before the detailed "What's New" / "What Changed" body. The reader has just learned *that* something changed; the cache-clear is the single mandatory action and must not be buried at the bottom of a long doc where it is easy to miss. (`release-notes/12.md` and `13.md` are worked examples — footer hoisted directly under the Summary.)
171	
172	Triggers — include the footer if the version added, removed, renamed, or modified any of:
173	
174	- A skill (`skills/<name>/SKILL.md` or its directory; on Cursor also `.cursor-skills/lr-<name>/`)
175	- A `/lr-*` slash command surface
176	- Any doc referenced by a SKILL.md whose runtime behavior is changed (the cache holds these too)
177	- A bundled script under `scripts/`
178	- A bundled MCP server (a root `.mcp.json` or an inline `mcpServers` block in `plugin.json`)
179	
180	Skip the footer only when the version is **purely informational or content-only** (lore-context conventions, doc rephrasing that doesn't change runtime behavior, framing-only release notes).
181	
182	Canonical wording (adapt as needed but keep the structure — substitute the `<...>` slots before publishing; use `release-notes/12.md` as a worked example):
183	
184	```markdown
185	## Clear Plugin Cache
186	
187	This release <added/removed/renamed> skills, so the existing plugin cache must be refreshed before Claude Code will see the changes.
188	
189	After applying the migration:
190	
191	1. `rm -rf ~/.claude/plugins/cache/lore-framework/` (targeted; preserves other plugins' caches), or `rm -rf ~/.claude/plugins/cache/` (broader fallback), or run the workspace's `./clear_plugin_cache.sh` if present.
192	2. Exit your current Claude Code session and start a new one.
193	3. Verify the <new/renamed> skill appears in the available-skills list.
194	
195	If you skip this step, the prior version's skill catalog will continue to load until the cache is refreshed. See `docs/doctor-stale-plugin-cache.md` for diagnosis.
196	```
197	
198	Rationale: the cache-stale failure mode is invisible until the user invokes a missing skill and gets confused. Authoring discipline puts the fix in the migration's own line of sight rather than relying on the user finding `/lr:doctor` after the fact.
199	
200	## Teammate Discipline
201	
202	`/lr:spawn-teammate` creates teammates **for the express purpose of giving the user a parallel pane to work with that agent directly**. The framework codifies this as an asymmetric two-sided discipline; this section is a maintainer's index, not a restatement.
203	
204	- **Teammate-side rules** — canonical source: `docs/teammate-conventions.md`. Loaded by `agent-boot.md` Step 2 on detected teammate spawns and adopted as standing rules for the session.
205	- **Lead-side redirect protocol** — canonical source: `docs/spawn-teammate.md` § *Lead behavior — teammate-to-lead messages*. When a teammate sends the lead a request that should have gone to the user, the lead redirects via SendMessage rather than relaying or acting on it.
206	
207	The asymmetry is intentional: spawning is one-directional (lead spawns teammate, never the inverse), so the rules differ. Both sides need explicit framework-level guidance because the natural Agent Teams default biases everyone toward team-lead-as-hub, which is the opposite of what spawn-teammate is trying to achieve.
208	
209	**Single source of truth, three loading sites.** The teammate-side rules are stated **once** in `teammate-conventions.md`. They reach the teammate's working memory via three sites, none of which restate the rules in full:
210	
211	1. `agent-boot.md` Step 2 — Reads the canonical doc and instructs the teammate to adopt it as standing rules.
212	2. `spawn-teammate.md` Step 6's spawn prompt — points to the boot-time load and includes a one-sentence recap as a fallback for the case where Step 2's load fails.
213	3. This § Teammate Discipline — pointers only.
214	
215	Drift hazard is bounded: only `teammate-conventions.md` carries the full RULE text, and the other sites only need updating if the *number* or *referencing* of rules changes, not their content.
216	
217	A maintainer reviewing or evolving any framework piece that touches the teammate boundary (`spawn-teammate.md`, `agent-boot.md` Step 2, `teammate-conventions.md`) should treat both sides as load-bearing and changes to one side as requiring a check on the other.
218	
219	## Migration Write Paths
220	
221	Every `migrations/<N>.md` must declare the set of repo-relative paths it may write under a `## Write Paths` section. The boot-time auto-upgrade gate (`docs/version-check.md` Step 1) intersects this set with the repo's dirty tracked files to decide whether to defer — uncommitted changes in files outside the write-set never block the upgrade.
222	
223	**Format.** Place a single fenced code block (` ``` … ``` `) immediately after the `## Write Paths` heading. The fenced block's content is the section's machine-readable body; everything outside the fence (prose paragraphs after the closing fence) is human commentary and is not parsed. Inside the fence:
224	
225	- One repo-relative glob per line. Use `**` for arbitrary depth, `*` for a single path segment.
226	- Comments after a `#` on the same line are allowed for clarity; lines starting with `#` are full-line comments.
227	- For migrations that genuinely write nothing, use the `(none)` sentinel as the body — see *Empty write-sets* below.
228	
229	Example:
230	
231	```markdown
232	## Write Paths
233	
234	```
235	agents/*/role.md                         # frontmatter rewrite
236	.claude/commands/lr-*-agent.md           # Claude boot-command regeneration
237	```
238	
239	(Optional human commentary after the closing fence is fine and is ignored by the parser.)
240	```
241	
242	Rules:
243	
244	- **Declare every path the migration may touch**, even paths that the migration only conditionally writes — the gate is conservative on declared collisions, not on actual ones.
245	- **Paths are repo-relative** to `<lore-agent-repo>` (the booting repo). The collision gate runs `git -C <lore-agent-repo> status --porcelain` and matches its output against the write-set globs, so paths must resolve inside the booting repo.
246	- **Do not include `lore-repo.md`** — the gate always adds it (the version stamp is universal).
247	- **Release-notes-only versions** have no migration file and no write-set; they contribute nothing to the gate.
248	- **Delete-only steps** (e.g. orphaned-file removal) do not require declaration. Write Paths is about *writes* — paths the migration may overwrite. A delete that the user has already confirmed cannot collide with itself, and declaring a delete-glob bloats the gate's collision surface (any dirty file matching the delete-glob would defer the upgrade even though no overwrite is at risk).
249	
250	### Known gap: workspace-root paths
251	
252	Some migrations write files outside the booting lore-agent repo — most notably
253	`.claude/commands/lr-*-agent.md` (Claude per-agent shortcuts at the workspace root),
254	`.cursor/skills/lr-*-agent/` (Cursor per-agent skills at the workspace root), and, on Codex,
255	`~/.codex/skills/lr-*-agent/` (personal-skill shortcuts). Because the gate is per-repo and these
256	locations sit outside the booting repo, **dirty edits there cannot enter the collision
257	intersection** and the gate cannot protect against them.
258	
259	This is a known gap, not a fix-by-misdeclaration: declaring `.claude/commands/...`,
260	`.cursor/skills/...`, or `~/.codex/skills/...` in a migration's Write Paths does not make the gate
261	see those files; it just bloats the write-set with paths that will never match. Migrations 5 and 6
262	historically write to `.claude/commands/`; a future shortcut-refresh migration would have the same
263	constraint for Cursor and Codex shortcut locations.
264	
265	The mitigation lives in the migrations themselves, not the gate: migrations 2/5/6 detect manual edits via known-template matching and present a three-way merge (see `update.md` § *Handling Manual Edits to Generated Files*). The gate doesn't replace that mechanism — it complements it for repo-local files. A future workspace-aware gate would close the hole; until then, treat workspace-root paths as protected by the in-migration three-way merge alone.
266	
267	If a migration ships without a `## Write Paths` section, the gate falls back to the conservative blanket-dirty rule for any version range that includes that migration. This is a real friction cost for users — every dirty file (including unrelated `workdir/*` runtime state) blocks the auto-upgrade. **Treat the section as mandatory** when authoring a new migration. `/lr:check` #20 enforces this automatically; reviewers should treat a check #20 failure as a blocker.
268	
269	### Glob token grammar
270	
271	Inside the fenced body, lines that aren't blank, comments, or the `(none)` sentinel are **glob tokens**. The canonical grammar (referenced verbatim by `version-check.md` Step 1b's parser and `check.md` #20.3's validator):
272	
273	- A glob token is a **single contiguous run of path-segment characters** with no internal whitespace. Accepted characters: `A-Z`, `a-z`, `0-9`, `.`, `_`, `-`, `/`, `*`, `?`, `[`, `]`, `!`, `\`.
274	- Use `**` for arbitrary path depth, `*` for a single path segment, `?` for any single character, `[...]` for a character class, `!` to negate a class, `\` to escape a literal special character.
275	- Optionally followed by whitespace and a `# <comment>` to end of line. The trailing comment is stripped; the token is what's left.
276	- A line containing **internal whitespace before any `#`** is prose, not a glob — the parser treats it as malformed (see `version-check.md` Step 1b on the parser's behavior; `check.md` #20.3 flags it as an error).
277	
278	Examples:
279	
280	```
281	agents/*/role.md                         # frontmatter rewrite (valid: single token + comment)
282	agents/**/*.md                           # arbitrary depth (valid)
283	.claude/commands/lr-[a-z]*-agent.md      # character class (valid)
284	agents/[!l]*/role.md                     # negated character class (valid)
285	```
286	
287	Non-examples (would be flagged):
288	
289	```
290	This migration writes role.md files     # internal whitespace, no path-token shape — prose
291	agents/*/role.md and lore-context.md     # two tokens on one line — split into two
292	```
293	
294	### Empty write-sets — sentinel forms
295	
296	If a migration genuinely writes nothing on disk (purely informational, all output is stdout), still include the `## Write Paths` section. The body must declare empty explicitly via one of these accepted forms (this is the canonical grammar — `version-check.md` Step 1b's parser and `check.md` #20.3's validator both reference this section):
297	
298	- **Bare sentinel:** a line containing only the literal token `(none)`.
299	- **Sentinel + prose:** a line starting with `(none)` followed by a space and any free-text comment. The separator can be a bare space, an ASCII hyphen `-`, or an em-dash `—` — all three are accepted.
300	- **Empty fenced block:** the fenced body contains zero lines after stripping blanks and `#`-comments.
301	
302	All three are equivalent: parser contributes nothing to the write-set; gate proceeds without deferral.
303	
304	Two example forms; pick whichever reads clearer:
305	
306	```markdown
307	## Write Paths
308	
309	```
310	(none) — this migration writes no files; all output is informational.
311	```
312	```
313	
314	or just an empty fenced block:
315	
316	```markdown
317	## Write Paths
318	
319	```
320	```
321	```
322	
323	Both declare an explicit empty write-set: the gate parses the body, finds no globs, contributes nothing to the intersection, and proceeds without deferral. This is **not** the same as omitting the `## Write Paths` heading entirely — an absent heading triggers the conservative blanket-dirty fallback (the gate cannot tell whether the author meant "writes nothing" or forgot to declare).
324	
325	## Plugin Manifest Versioning
326	
327	Every `VERSION` bump must also bump the plugin manifest version in **all four** version-bearing manifests:
328	
329	- `.claude-plugin/plugin.json`
330	- `.claude-plugin/marketplace.json` (the `lr` plugin entry)
331	- `.cursor-plugin/plugin.json`
332	- `.codex-plugin/plugin.json`
333	
334	(The Codex native marketplace file `.agents/plugins/marketplace.json` carries no per-plugin `version` field — Codex reads the plugin version from `.codex-plugin/plugin.json` — so it is not part of this set.)
335	
336	Use the mechanical mapping **`"version": "1.<VERSION>.0"`** (framework `VERSION` 14 → manifest `1.14.0`) — derived from `VERSION` so it can't be forgotten, strictly increasing, and valid semver. `/lr:check` enforces this (check #19): it flags any manifest whose version isn't `1.<VERSION>.0`.
337	
338	Why it matters for Claude Code: Claude identifies a plugin release by the manifest version. If the manifest stays frozen (it sat at `1.0.0` from v1 through v13), the plugin layer never sees a new version and never refreshes its cache on its own — the root cause of the recurring "stale skill catalog after an upgrade" pain that the manual **Clear Plugin Cache** footer exists to work around.
339	
340	The `.cursor-plugin/plugin.json` bump is **consistency hygiene / mechanical parity** with `VERSION` — do **not** claim it is a verified Cursor cache-detection lever (that mechanism is verified for Claude Code only). It still must move with every `VERSION` bump so mixed-engine checkouts stay internally consistent. The `.codex-plugin/plugin.json` bump **is** the version Codex reads for the plugin (verified against `codex plugin add`); for a git marketplace install `codex plugin marketplace upgrade` picks up the bumped version.
341	
342	Relationship to the cache-clear footer: bumping the Claude manifests is what lets the platform *detect* a release; keep the Clear Plugin Cache footer (above) as belt-and-suspenders until it's confirmed that a manifest bump alone makes Claude Code auto-invalidate the cache. The two are complementary. See `doctor-stale-plugin-cache.md`.
343	
344	## Plugin MCP Servers
345	
346	The plugin can bundle MCP servers that Claude Code (the MCP host) launches automatically when the plugin is enabled, exposing their tools to the agent. Declare them in a **`.mcp.json` at the plugin root** (keeps `plugin.json` minimal; an inline `mcpServers` block in `plugin.json` is the equivalent alternative). `<framework-root>` expands inside `command`/`args`/`env`/`cwd`, so reference bundled files as `<framework-root>/scripts/<name>`. `scripts/wait-server.py` (server `lr-wait`, see `wait.md`) is the first instance. Two ship-ceremony consequences: adding, renaming, or removing a server is **cache-affecting** (carry the Clear Plugin Cache footer, as for a skill), and the server's runtime language follows § Tooling: Non-Shell Runtimes.
347	
348	## Dev-Only Artifacts
349	
350	Tests, benchmarks, and other development-only artifacts do **not** ship in the plugin repo — it is the distributed marketplace artifact and stays slim. They live in the framework-dev repo (`lore-framework-dev/`, e.g. `tests/`) and reference the plugin as a workspace sibling (default `../lore-framework`, override `$LR_FRAMEWORK_DIR`). This extends the `plugin-vs-agent-repo-separation` principle: plugin repo = what's distributed; dev repo = how it's built and verified.
351	
````

Ten sections, in order:

1. **Naming** — kebab-case rules for agents, topics, skills, plus the per-engine command prefix (`/lr:`, `/lr-`, `$lr-`).
2. **Placeholder Vocabulary** — what `<workspace>`, `<lore-agent-repo>`, `<agent-name>`, etc. mean.
3. **Directory Structure** — the standard shape of an agent repo and a workspace, as a tree diagram.
4. **Descriptor Files** — the YAML frontmatter rules for `lore-repo.md`, `lore-workspace.md`, `role.md`.
5. **Lore Topics** / **Lore Context** — what makes a good lore file, and what `lore-context.md` should (and shouldn't) contain.
6. **What Belongs / Doesn't Belong in Lore** — a short checklist.
7. **Tooling** — three subsections: CWD safety (`git -C` instead of `cd`), portable shell (no GNU-only tools like `timeout`), and when `python3` is allowed.
8. **Script Fallback Contract** — the one we just discussed.
9. **Migration / Release-Note Authoring** — the Clear Plugin Cache footer rule.
10. **Teammate Discipline, Migration Write Paths, Plugin Manifest Versioning, Plugin MCP Servers, Dev-Only Artifacts** — smaller reference sections, each a pointer to its own canonical doc, not full rules restated here.

So it's really a grab-bag reference doc — one section per convention, each usually pointing further out rather than holding the full rule itself.

### 66. Assistant — 2026-07-26T00:17:50.107Z

- model: `claude-sonnet-5`

Here's the whole thing in one short version:

**The problem:** Boot, attach, consult, recall, and pull-lore used to be prose. The agent read instructions and did each small step itself — one tool call per step (find the repo, run git pull, compare versions, check for a teammate spawn...).

**The change:** Those mechanical steps now live in one script, `scripts/lr-core`. It has three commands:
- `preflight` — does the whole boot check in one shot (find agent, pull repo, compare version, detect teammate)
- `scan` — lists an agent's lore topics with title + age (used by recall)
- `discover` — lists all agents in the workspace

So instead of many small steps, the agent runs one command and gets one JSON answer back.

**The safety net:** If the script breaks, the agent doesn't just get stuck. It falls back to doing the old prose steps by hand — that's the Script Fallback Contract we just went through.

**Status:** Built, reviewed twice, but not shipped yet. Parked on its own branch after a rough session on 2026-07-25.

Want to go deeper on any one part, or something else?

### 67. User — 2026-07-26T00:22:35.758Z

what is stated in Script Fallback Contract?

### 68. Assistant — 2026-07-26T00:22:40.805Z

- model: `claude-sonnet-5`

We already went through this in detail earlier — here's the short version again:

A shipped script is one of two kinds, and that decides what happens when it breaks:

- **Accelerator** (like `lr-core`) — a prose procedure also exists for it. If the script fails, the agent tells the user in one line, then does the steps by hand instead. The flow still finishes.
- **Implementation** (like `lrb.py`, `workspace-pull`, `sync-cursor-skills`) — there is no prose version, the script **is** the procedure. If it fails, the agent stops that operation and reports the error — it must not improvise a fake substitute.

One more rule: if the script runs fine but *reports* a problem (like "pull failed" inside its JSON output), that's not a script failure — that's just data. No fallback needed there.

Want more, or move on?

### 78. Assistant — 2026-07-26T00:27:03.620Z

- model: `claude-sonnet-5`

Short answer: we can slim the prose, but making the script the *only* source of truth has a real cost — worth flagging before we do it.

Here's why. Right now `lr-core` is called an **accelerator**: the prose is normative, the script just executes it faster. That's why boot never truly breaks — if `python3` is missing, or the script has a bug, the agent falls back to the prose and boot still works.

If we flip it — script as source of truth, prose thinned to almost nothing — `lr-core` becomes an **implementation** script (like `lrb.py`). Then if it fails, there's nothing to fall back to. Boot could just stop working on a machine without `python3`, or when the script breaks.

Two ways to slim it without losing that safety net:

1. **Compress the wording, keep the steps.** Same procedure, fewer words — cut restatement, tighten sentences. Safe, no design change.
2. **Flip to script-as-truth.** Genuinely shorter doc, but boot loses its manual fallback. Only reasonable if we're fine with "boot may just fail" as an acceptable risk.

Which one do you want?

`.worktrees/lore-framework/lr-core-v31/`) mechanizes what used to be prose-executed boot/attach/
consult/pull-lore/process-merge/lore-search steps. Today it is classified as an **Accelerator** in

doc, and that doc, not the script, is normative."* Concretely this means the exact same procedure
is written out twice — once as executable Python, once as hand-executable prose in `agent-boot.md`
§ Manual Boot Procedure, `auto-pull.md` Steps 1–3, and shorter restated fallback clauses inside
`attach.md`, `consult.md`, `lore-search.md`, `process-merge.md`, `pull-lore.md`. Confirmed
duplication (from direct research today): `agent-boot.md`'s Manual Boot Procedure literally
labels itself "the normative specification of what preflight does," restating the same four
operations preflight performs in one call.

The user's request: flip this. The script's own source — specifically, instructional comments
written *as steps to follow*, not as code annotations — becomes the normative procedure. The
separate prose duplicates get thinned to short pointers. When the script can't run, the agent
reads the relevant function in `scripts/lr-core` and executes its commented steps by hand — the
code (with its comments) is a *more* precise fallback spec than prose, not a weaker one, because
it can't silently drift out of sync with what actually runs. This also resolves the earlier
"boot could just stop working" worry: the fallback isn't discarded, it moves from a separate
doc into the script itself, right next to the logic it describes, which is the tightest possible
place to keep procedure and implementation from diverging (single canonical source, one home
instead of two).

The user also wants this named as a reusable framework design principle, not a one-off fix for
`lr-core`.

**Explicitly out of scope:** no behavioral/logic changes to the script. Every edit is comments and
prose — `cmd_preflight`, `cmd_discover`, `cmd_scan`, `pull_repo`, `compare_versions`,
`detect_teammate` keep their exact current code. `tests/test_lr_core.py` needs no changes and must
still pass unmodified. Nothing gets committed as part of this change — edits land in the existing
worktree working tree only (the branch already has one uncommitted-intent WIP commit; how/when to
commit is a separate later decision).

## Approach

### 1. `scripts/lr-core` — add step-instruction comments; flip the module docstring's claim

File: `.worktrees/lore-framework/lr-core-v31/scripts/lr-core`

- **Module docstring (lines 1–47).** Currently states "WHAT THIS IS NOT: procedure docs remain
  normative." Replace with the new framing: this script's own step comments *are* the normative
  procedure; `docs/*.md` files carry short pointers into specific functions, not full restatements.
  Keep the parts that are still true as-is: judgment (interpreting `role.md`/`lore-context.md`,
  deciding what a version-skew or teammate detection *means*) stays with the agent — the script
  only mechanizes the deterministic legwork.
- **`cmd_preflight` (lines 719–773).** This is the orchestrator with the least commenting today —
  confirmed by research: exactly one inline comment in ~55 lines, while the helpers it calls
  (`pull_repo`, `compare_versions`, `detect_teammate`) already carry good rationale comments. Add a
  numbered `# Step N:` comment above each logical block, written as an instruction a reader could
  follow without running the code:
  1. Resolve `<framework-root>` and read its `VERSION`.
  2. Resolve the target agent (delegates to `_resolve_agent` — note in the comment what that
     helper does: `--agent-dir` direct use vs. workspace-wide `discover_workspace` scan, and the
     no-match / ambiguous-match handling).
  3. Record `role.md` / `lore-context.md` paths into `read_next` — keep the existing comment
     explaining these are for the *caller* to read and interpret, not the script.
  4. If the agent sits under a real lore repo: auto-pull it (TTL-cached — point at `pull_repo`'s
     own comments for the exact git invocation and TTL file), then compare its version stamp
     against `VERSION` (point at `compare_versions`).
  5. Otherwise (no enclosing repo): note explicitly that pull/version are hardcoded to
     `skipped`/`unknown` and no subprocess runs.
  6. Detect Agent-Teams teammate spawn (point at `detect_teammate`'s own comments for the exact
     `ps` invocation and the `--agent-id` matching rule), unless suppressed.
- **`cmd_discover` (662–679) and `cmd_scan` (776–806)** — same treatment: a short `# Step N:`
  comment per block. `cmd_scan` already has two decent comments near the end; leave those, add
  the missing ones earlier in the function.
- **`pull_repo`, `compare_versions`, `detect_teammate`, `_resolve_agent`.** These already contain
  good rationale comments (bare-repo edge case, GNU-`timeout` avoidance, `ANCESTOR_SCAN_DEPTH`
  reasoning, etc.) — light pass only: make sure each function's *opening* comment states the exact
  hand-executable procedure a person would need (exact command with flags, exact file path for the
  TTL stamp, the fresh-vs-ttl decision rule, the `--agent-id` regex boundary rule) so nothing is
  missing if a caller lands here directly from a doc pointer instead of from `cmd_preflight`.
  Reframe tone where needed from "why we did this" to "do this — here's why," but don't rewrite
  content that's already correct.

### 2. `docs/conventions.md` — redefine the Accelerator category

File: `.worktrees/lore-framework/lr-core-v31/docs/conventions.md`, § Script Fallback Contract
(lines 145–166) and the `lr-core` mention in § Tooling: Non-Shell Runtimes (139–143).

- Redefine **Accelerator** (only `lr-core` has ever been an instance of this category — no other
  accelerator scripts exist today, so this is a redefinition in place, not a new third category):
  running code accelerates a procedure, but the procedure lives in **the script's own instructional
  comments**, not a separate prose doc. A doc that delegates to it carries only a short pointer:
  what to call, what the output fields mean, and — on failure — *which function* to go read.
  **Implementation** stays as-is (`workspace-pull`, `session-takeover`, `sync-cursor-skills`,
  `lr-emit`/`wait-server.py`, `lrb.py` — nothing about these changes).
- On-failure instruction changes from "execute the canonical prose procedure the doc points to"
  to "read the named function(s) in the script and execute their commented steps by hand."
- Name the general pattern explicitly in this section (a couple of sentences): comments-as-spec
  for an accelerator script means procedure and implementation can't drift apart into two versions
  of the truth, and the fallback is exactly as current as the code that just failed. This is the
  paragraph the user asked to have stated as a design principle — see item 6 below for where it
  also gets a durable lore home.

### 3. `docs/agent-boot.md` — replace Manual Boot Procedure with a pointer

File: `.worktrees/lore-framework/lr-core-v31/docs/agent-boot.md`

- Delete the hand-executed restatement in § Manual Boot Procedure (lines 76–92, currently labeled
  "the normative specification of what preflight does" — that claim moves to the script).
- Replace with a short paragraph: on preflight failure, apply the Script Fallback Contract by
  reading `cmd_preflight` (and the helpers it calls) in `scripts/lr-core`, executing each commented
  step by hand, in order, then resuming at Step 3 (read `role.md`/`lore-context.md`) and Step 4
  (confirm) exactly as today.
- Steps 1–4 (the script-driven happy path, lines 26–93) stay as-is — they already just call
  preflight and act on its JSON.

### 4. `docs/auto-pull.md` — same pattern, biggest single trim

File: `.worktrees/lore-framework/lr-core-v31/docs/auto-pull.md` (100 lines; Steps 1–3 span
lines 24–66 and duplicate `pull_repo()` almost exactly: bare-repo handling, `GIT_TERMINAL_PROMPT`,
SSH batch mode, the `.git/lr-last-pull` TTL file).

- Replace the full Steps 1–3 hand-procedure with a pointer to `pull_repo()`'s comments in
  `scripts/lr-core` as the normative spec.
- Keep the non-duplicated conceptual sections as-is: Inputs, Worktrees, Invariants, Distinct From,
  See Also — these aren't step-by-step procedure, they're framing that doesn't live in the script.
- Update line 14's "This doc remains normative" framing to match the flip.

### 5. `attach.md`, `consult.md`, `lore-search.md`, `process-merge.md`, `pull-lore.md` — thin the fallback pointers

Each currently restates a mini hand-procedure inline in its Script Fallback Contract sentence
(confirmed exact quotes from research, e.g. `attach.md` line 54 spells out the full
discover→pull→compare sequence again). Replace each with a one-line pointer naming the specific
`scripts/lr-core` function(s) relevant to that call site (e.g. `attach.md` → `_resolve_agent` +
`pull_repo` + `compare_versions`; `consult.md` → `discover_workspace`; `lore-search.md` →
`scan_lore`; `process-merge.md` / `pull-lore.md` → `pull_repo` via `cmd_preflight`). This also
removes the mild single-canonical-source violation where each site independently restates its own
version of the same fallback text.

### 6. Name the principle as lore (per the user's explicit ask)

New topic in `lore-framework-dev/agents/lore-architect/lore/` (worktree
`.worktrees/lore-framework-dev/lr-core-v31/`), e.g. `literate-accelerator-pattern.md`: what the
pattern is (instructional comments inside an accelerator script serve as the procedure spec, not
just code annotation), the problem it solves (prose/code drift; a broken script losing its
fallback), the one rule that makes it work (comments must read as freestanding instructions a
reader could execute without running the code — not "what this code does" but "do this"), and the
first instance (`scripts/lr-core`, v31). One-line addition to `lore-context.md` § Skills & Docs
pointing at the new topic, matching how other summary topics are referenced there.

### 7. `release-notes/31.md` — align with the new framing

File: `.worktrees/lore-framework/lr-core-v31/release-notes/31.md`. It currently describes
`lr-core` under the old "prose is normative" framing (Script Fallback Contract paragraph, and the
"What Changed" bullets describing docs as keeping full manual procedures). Update those paragraphs
to describe the new shape: docs carry pointers, the script's comments carry the procedure.

1	#!/usr/bin/env python3
2	"""lr-core — deterministic substrate for the mechanical steps of lore skills.
3	
4	Part of the lore-framework plugin (`lr`). Pure Python 3 standard library, no
5	pip install, one file — the same sanctioned-exception class as `scripts/lrb.py`
6	and `scripts/wait-server.py` (see docs/conventions.md § Tooling: Non-Shell
7	Runtimes).
8	
9	WHAT THIS IS. Boot, attach, consult, recall and finalize each carry a large
10	mechanical half — scan the workspace, pull a repo, compare a version stamp,
11	detect a teammate spawn, enumerate lore topics — that was historically executed
12	as prose by the model, one tool call per step. That work needs no judgment, so
13	it belongs in code: it runs in one round-trip instead of six, it can't drift
14	between engines, and it encodes the platform traps (macOS `ps` field packing,
15	missing GNU `timeout`, HTTPS credential prompts) once instead of in every doc.
16	
17	WHAT THIS IS NOT. The procedure docs remain the normative specification; this
18	script is the fast path over them. Anything requiring judgment stays with the
19	model: applying migrations, deciding what lore means, composing summaries,
20	resolving conflicts. See docs/conventions.md § Script Fallback Contract — when
21	this script cannot complete, the caller notifies the user and executes the
22	prose procedure by hand.
23	
24	SUBCOMMANDS
25	  discover    Enumerate lore agent repos and their agents in a workspace.
26	  preflight   Boot/attach/consult preflight for one agent: resolve, pull
27	              (TTL-cached), compare versions, detect teammate spawn.
28	  scan        Manifest of an agent's lore topics with git dates and staleness.
29	
30	OUTPUT CONTRACT. Every subcommand prints exactly one JSON object:
31	
32	    {"ok": bool, "data": {...}, "warnings": [str], "errors": [str]}
33	
34	  `ok`   — was the request satisfied? A determinate negative answer (no such
35	           agent) is ok:false with exit 0: the script functioned, the caller
36	           acts on `errors`/`data` per its procedure doc. No takeover needed.
37	
38	EXIT CODES
39	  0  The script ran to completion; `data` is authoritative. Degraded conditions
40	     (pull failed, `ps` blocked) are reported *inside* data — they are results,
41	     not failures, exactly as the prose procedures treat them.
42	  2  The script could not complete. The caller must fall back to the prose
43	     procedure per the Script Fallback Contract. Callers treat any unexpected
44	     exit code, absent output, or unparsable output as 2 as well.
45	
46	Floor: Python 3.9. No `match`, no `X | Y` annotations.
47	"""
48	
49	import argparse
50	import json
51	import os
52	import re
53	import subprocess
54	import sys
55	import time
56	
57	# Bound every git subprocess. The Bash-tool timeout that bounds prose-driven
58	# pulls does not exist here, so the script owns its own backstop.
59	GIT_TIMEOUT_SEC = 60
60	PS_TIMEOUT_SEC = 10
61	# `run()` returns this when a command never executed — missing binary, broken
62	# PATH, or a timeout. Distinct from any real exit status of the command itself.
63	GIT_UNRUNNABLE = -1
64	DEFAULT_PULL_TTL_SEC = 600
65	DEFAULT_STALE_DAYS = 180
66	# How far up the process tree to look for the Agent-Teams marker. The engine is
67	# typically the grandparent (script -> shell -> engine); the extra headroom
68	# covers wrapper scripts.
69	ANCESTOR_SCAN_DEPTH = 6
70	TEAMMATE_MARKER = "--agent-id"
71	# Flag-boundary match, not a substring test: `--agent-idle-timeout=30` in some
72	# unrelated ancestor must not read as the Agent-Teams marker.
73	TEAMMATE_MARKER_RE = re.compile(r"(?:^|\s)--agent-id(?:[\s=]|$)")
74	TITLE_MAX_CHARS = 120
75	
76	
77	# --------------------------------------------------------------------------
78	# Result plumbing
79	# --------------------------------------------------------------------------
80	
81	class Result(object):
82	    """Accumulates the single JSON object a subcommand prints."""
83	
84	    def __init__(self):
85	        self.ok = True
86	        self.data = {}
87	        self.warnings = []
88	        self.errors = []
89	
90	    def warn(self, msg):
91	        self.warnings.append(msg)
92	
93	    def fail(self, msg):
94	        """A determinate negative answer. Still exit 0 — the script functioned."""
95	        self.ok = False
96	        self.errors.append(msg)
97	
98	    def emit(self):
99	        payload = {
100	            "ok": self.ok,
101	            "data": self.data,
102	            "warnings": self.warnings,
103	            "errors": self.errors,
104	        }
105	        sys.stdout.write(json.dumps(payload, indent=2, sort_keys=False) + "\n")
106	        return 0
107	
108	
109	def emit_fatal(msg):
110	    """The script could not complete: exit 2, caller falls back to prose."""
111	    payload = {"ok": False, "data": {}, "warnings": [], "errors": [msg]}
112	    sys.stdout.write(json.dumps(payload, indent=2) + "\n")
113	    return 2

116	# --------------------------------------------------------------------------

120	def read_text(path):
121	    try:
122	        with open(path, "r", encoding="utf-8", errors="replace") as fh:
123	            return fh.read()
124	    except (IOError, OSError):
125	        return None
126	
127	
128	def run(argv, timeout, env_extra=None, cwd=None):
129	    """Run a command. Returns (rc, stdout, stderr); rc -1 on timeout/missing binary.
130	
131	    Never raises — a missing binary or a hung remote is data for the caller,
132	    not an exception that aborts a boot.
133	    """
134	    env = os.environ.copy()
135	    if env_extra:
136	        env.update(env_extra)
137	    try:
138	        proc = subprocess.run(
139	            argv,
140	            stdout=subprocess.PIPE,
141	            stderr=subprocess.PIPE,
142	            timeout=timeout,
143	            env=env,
144	            cwd=cwd,
145	        )
146	        return (
147	            proc.returncode,
148	            proc.stdout.decode("utf-8", "replace"),
149	            proc.stderr.decode("utf-8", "replace"),
150	        )
151	    except subprocess.TimeoutExpired:
152	        return (-1, "", "timed out after %ss" % timeout)
153	    except (OSError, ValueError) as exc:
154	        return (-1, "", str(exc))
155	
156	
157	def _git_unrunnable(err):
158	    """The uniform result for a git call that never executed.
159	
160	    Applies at EVERY git call site, not just the first one. `git` failing to run
161	    (absent binary, broken PATH, timeout) is not the same answer as git running
162	    and saying no — collapsing the two lets a broken toolchain masquerade as a
163	    legitimate "not a repo" / "no origin remote" skip, under an exit code that
164	    promises the data is authoritative.
165	    """
166	    return {"status": "failed",
167	            "detail": "could not run git: %s" % (err.strip()[:200] or "unknown")}
168	
169	
170	def git(repo, args, timeout=GIT_TIMEOUT_SEC, env_extra=None):
171	    """Run git against `repo` with -C. Never `cd` — the shell CWD is shared
172	    with the caller's other tools (docs/conventions.md § Tooling: CWD Safety).
173	
174	    Every invocation is forced to the C locale: some outcomes are detected by
175	    reading git's human-readable stdout, which is translated under an NLS-
176	    enabled git in a non-English locale.
177	    """
178	    env = {"LC_ALL": "C", "LANGUAGE": "C"}
179	    if env_extra:
180	        env.update(env_extra)
181	    return run(["git", "-C", repo] + args, timeout, env_extra=env)
182	
183	
184	def parse_frontmatter(text):
185	    """Parse the minimal YAML subset the framework uses in descriptor files.
186	
187	    Supports `key: value` (optionally quoted) and block sequences of the form
188	    `key:` followed by `  - item` lines. Not a general YAML parser and does not
189	    need to be — descriptor schemas are fixed by docs/conventions.md.
190	    """
191	    out = {}
192	    if not text:
193	        return out
194	    lines = text.split("\n")
195	    if not lines or lines[0].strip() != "---":
196	        return out
197	    body = []
198	    for line in lines[1:]:
199	        if line.strip() == "---":
200	            break
201	        body.append(line)
202	    current_list_key = None
203	    for line in body:
204	        if not line.strip() or line.lstrip().startswith("#"):
205	            continue
206	        item = re.match(r"^\s+-\s+(.*)$", line)
207	        if item and current_list_key:
208	            out[current_list_key].append(_unquote(item.group(1).strip()))
209	            continue
210	        kv = re.match(r"^([A-Za-z0-9_-]+):\s*(.*)$", line)
211	        if not kv:
212	            continue
213	        key, val = kv.group(1), kv.group(2).strip()
214	        if val == "":
215	            out[key] = []
216	            current_list_key = key
217	        else:
218	            out[key] = _unquote(val)
219	            current_list_key = None
220	    return out
221	
222	
223	def _unquote(val):
224	    if len(val) >= 2 and val[0] == val[-1] and val[0] in ("'", '"'):
225	        return val[1:-1]
226	    return val
227	
228	
229	def resolve_framework_root(explicit):
230	    """Framework root = the directory containing VERSION.
231	
232	    Defaults to this script's parent directory (scripts/ lives at the root).
233	    Boot Step 0 resolves the root for the *engine*; this is the script's own
234	    independent resolution so it can be run standalone.
235	    """
236	    if explicit:
237	        return os.path.abspath(explicit)
238	    return os.path.dirname(os.path.dirname(os.path.realpath(__file__)))
239	
240	
241	def framework_version(root):
242	    text = read_text(os.path.join(root, "VERSION"))
243	    return text.strip() if text else None
244	
245	
246	# --------------------------------------------------------------------------
247	# Discovery
248	# --------------------------------------------------------------------------
249	
250	def find_repos(workspace):
251	    """Top-level dirs of `workspace` containing lore-repo.md.
252	
253	    Top-level only, deliberately: nested repos are invisible to the framework
254	    (docs/conventions.md § Directory Structure). Hidden dirs are skipped, which
255	    also excludes the .worktrees/ convention.
256	    """
257	    repos = []
258	    try:
259	        entries = sorted(os.listdir(workspace))
260	    except (IOError, OSError):
261	        return repos
262	    for name in entries:
263	        if name.startswith("."):
264	            continue
265	        path = os.path.join(workspace, name)
266	        if not os.path.isdir(path):
267	            continue
268	        descriptor = os.path.join(path, "lore-repo.md")
269	        if os.path.isfile(descriptor):
270	            repos.append(path)
271	    return repos
272	
273	
274	def describe_repo(repo_path):
275	    fm = parse_frontmatter(read_text(os.path.join(repo_path, "lore-repo.md")))
276	    repos_field = fm.get("repos", [])
277	    if isinstance(repos_field, str):
278	        repos_field = [repos_field] if repos_field else []
279	    return {
280	        "path": repo_path,
281	        "name": os.path.basename(repo_path),
282	        "description": fm.get("description"),
283	        "version": fm.get("version"),
284	        "repos": repos_field,
285	    }
286	
287	
288	def repo_agents(repo_path):
289	    """Agents in one repo: agents/<name>/ containing role.md."""
290	    agents = []
291	    agents_dir = os.path.join(repo_path, "agents")
292	    if not os.path.isdir(agents_dir):
293	        return agents
294	    try:
295	        names = sorted(os.listdir(agents_dir))
296	    except (IOError, OSError):
297	        return agents
298	    for name in names:
299	        if name.startswith("."):
300	            continue
301	        agent_dir = os.path.join(agents_dir, name)
302	        role = os.path.join(agent_dir, "role.md")
303	        if not os.path.isfile(role):
304	            continue
305	        fm = parse_frontmatter(read_text(role))
306	        agents.append({
307	            "name": name,
308	            "dir": agent_dir,
309	            "repo": repo_path,
310	            "description": fm.get("description"),
311	            "has_lore_context": os.path.isfile(
312	                os.path.join(agent_dir, "lore-context.md")),
313	        })
314	    return agents
315	
316	
317	def discover_workspace(workspace):
318	    repos, agents = [], []
319	    for repo_path in find_repos(workspace):
320	        info = describe_repo(repo_path)
321	        found = repo_agents(repo_path)
322	        info["agents"] = [a["name"] for a in found]
323	        repos.append(info)
324	        agents.extend(found)
325	    return repos, agents
326	
327	
328	# --------------------------------------------------------------------------
329	# Git: TTL-cached auto-pull
330	# --------------------------------------------------------------------------
331	
332	def absolute_git_dir(repo):
333	    rc, out, _ = git(repo, ["rev-parse", "--absolute-git-dir"], timeout=15)
334	    if rc == 0 and out.strip():
335	        return out.strip()
336	    return None
337	
338	
339	def _stamp_path(repo):
340	    gd = absolute_git_dir(repo)
341	    if not gd:
342	        return None
343	    # Inside the git dir: never committed, no .gitignore change needed, and it
344	    # follows the checkout when the worktree convention moves it.
345	    return os.path.join(gd, "lr-last-pull")
346	
347	
348	def _read_stamp(repo):
349	    path = _stamp_path(repo)
350	    if not path:
351	        return None
352	    text = read_text(path)
353	    if not text:
354	        return None
355	    try:
356	        return float(text.strip())
357	    except ValueError:
358	        return None
359	
360	
361	def _write_stamp(repo):
362	    path = _stamp_path(repo)
363	    if not path:
364	        return
365	    try:
366	        with open(path, "w", encoding="utf-8") as fh:
367	            fh.write("%d\n" % int(time.time()))
368	    except (IOError, OSError):
369	        pass  # A stamp we cannot write only costs us the TTL optimization.
370	
371	
372	def pull_repo(repo, ttl=DEFAULT_PULL_TTL_SEC, fresh=False, do_pull=True):
373	    """Auto-pull one repo per docs/auto-pull.md, with a TTL cache.
374	
375	    Returns a dict; never raises. Statuses:
376	      disabled     — caller passed --no-pull
377	      fresh        — skipped, pulled within the TTL window
378	      up-to-date   — pull ran, nothing to fetch
379	      pulled       — fast-forwarded (commits counted when derivable)
380	      skipped      — not a git repo, or no origin remote (legitimate states)
381	      failed       — network/auth/non-fast-forward; caller continues degraded
382	    """
383	    if not do_pull:
384	        return {"status": "disabled", "detail": "pull suppressed by --no-pull"}
385	
386	    rc, out, err = git(repo, ["rev-parse", "--is-inside-work-tree"], timeout=15)
387	    if rc == GIT_UNRUNNABLE:
388	        return _git_unrunnable(err)
389	    if rc != 0:
390	        return {"status": "skipped", "detail": "not a git repo"}
391	    if out.strip() == "false":
392	        # Exits 0 inside a bare repo's own directory, but there is no work tree.
393	        return {"status": "skipped", "detail": "bare repository (no work tree)"}
394	
395	    rc, _, err = git(repo, ["remote", "get-url", "origin"], timeout=15)
396	    if rc == GIT_UNRUNNABLE:
397	        return _git_unrunnable(err)
398	    if rc != 0:
399	        return {"status": "skipped", "detail": "no origin remote"}
400	
401	    if not fresh and ttl > 0:
402	        stamp = _read_stamp(repo)
403	        if stamp is not None:
404	            age = time.time() - stamp
405	            if 0 <= age < ttl:
406	                return {
407	                    "status": "fresh",
408	                    "detail": "pulled %ds ago (ttl %ds)" % (int(age), ttl),
409	                    "age_seconds": int(age),
410	                }
411	
412	    # Per-transport fast-fail niceties over the subprocess-timeout backstop.
413	    # GNU `timeout` is deliberately not used — it is absent on macOS/BSD
414	    # (docs/conventions.md § Tooling: Portable Shell).
415	    env = {
416	        "GIT_TERMINAL_PROMPT": "0",
417	        "GIT_SSH_COMMAND": "ssh -o BatchMode=yes -o ConnectTimeout=10",
418	    }
419	    rc, out, err = git(repo, ["pull", "--ff-only"], env_extra=env)
420	    if rc != 0:
421	        detail = (err or out or "unknown error").strip().split("\n")
422	        return {"status": "failed", "detail": detail[-1][:400]}
423	
424	    _write_stamp(repo)
425	
426	    if "Already up to date" in out or "Already up-to-date" in out:
427	        return {"status": "up-to-date", "detail": "already up to date"}
428	
429	    commits = None
430	    crc, cout, _ = git(repo, ["rev-list", "HEAD@{1}..HEAD", "--count"], timeout=15)
431	    if crc == 0 and cout.strip().isdigit():
432	        commits = int(cout.strip())
433	    result = {"status": "pulled", "detail": "fast-forwarded"}
434	    if commits:
435	        result["commits"] = commits
436	        result["detail"] = "fast-forwarded %d commit(s)" % commits
437	    return result
438	
439	
440	# --------------------------------------------------------------------------
441	# Version comparison (detection only — migrations stay with the model)
442	# --------------------------------------------------------------------------
443	
444	def compare_versions(repo_version, fw_version):
445	    """Detect skew. Applying migrations is judgment work: docs/version-check.md."""
446	    out = {"repo": repo_version, "framework": fw_version}
447	    if repo_version is None or fw_version is None:
448	        out["verdict"] = "unknown"
449	        out["detail"] = "missing or unreadable version stamp"
450	        return out
451	    if str(repo_version).strip() == str(fw_version).strip():
452	        out["verdict"] = "match"
453	        return out
454	    try:
455	        rv, fv = int(str(repo_version).strip()), int(str(fw_version).strip())
456	    except ValueError:
457	        out["verdict"] = "differs"
458	        out["detail"] = "non-numeric version stamp(s); compare manually"
459	        return out
460	    out["verdict"] = "repo-behind" if rv < fv else "repo-ahead"
461	    return out
462	
463	
464	# --------------------------------------------------------------------------
465	# Teammate detection
466	# --------------------------------------------------------------------------
467	
468	def _ps_field(pid, field):
469	    """Read one ps field for one pid.
470	
471	    One field per call, deliberately: `ps -o ppid=,args=` packs multiple fields
472	    onto a single line, which has already produced a real parsing bug in this
473	    codebase (lore: macos-ps-o-multi-field-single-line.md). Trailing `=`
474	    suppresses the header.
475	    """
476	    rc, out, _ = run(["ps", "-o", field + "=", "-p", str(pid)], PS_TIMEOUT_SEC)
477	    if rc != 0:
478	        return None
479	    val = out.strip()
480	    return val if val else None
481	
482	
483	def detect_teammate():
484	    """Look for the Agent-Teams marker in this process's ancestry.
485	
486	    Returns verdict yes/no/unknown. `unknown` is a first-class answer: sandboxed
487	    engines block `ps`, and the profiles' capability gates expect that.
488	    """
489	    pid = os.getppid()
490	    depth = 0
491	    scanned = 0
492	    while pid and pid > 1 and depth < ANCESTOR_SCAN_DEPTH:
493	        args = _ps_field(pid, "args")
494	        if args is None:
495	            break
496	        scanned += 1
497	        if TEAMMATE_MARKER_RE.search(args):
498	            # Only the matching process is echoed back. The full ancestry is
499	            # noise the caller pays input tokens for; brevity is the point of
500	            # moving this off the prose path in the first place.
501	            return {
502	                "verdict": "yes",
503	                "detail": "found %s in ancestor pid %d" % (TEAMMATE_MARKER, pid),
504	                "matched": {"pid": pid, "args": args[:200]},
505	                "scanned": scanned,
506	            }
507	        parent = _ps_field(pid, "ppid")
508	        if not parent or not parent.strip().isdigit():
509	            break
510	        nxt = int(parent.strip())
511	        if nxt == pid:
512	            break
513	        pid = nxt
514	        depth += 1
515	    if not scanned:
516	        return {
517	            "verdict": "unknown",
518	            "detail": "ps unavailable or blocked; assume host session",
519	            "scanned": 0,
520	        }
521	    return {
522	        "verdict": "no",
523	        "detail": "no %s in %d ancestor(s)" % (TEAMMATE_MARKER, scanned),
524	        "scanned": scanned,
525	    }
526	
527	
528	# --------------------------------------------------------------------------
529	# Lore topic scan
530	# --------------------------------------------------------------------------
531	
532	def topic_title(path):
533	    text = read_text(path)
534	    if not text:
535	        return None
536	    for line in text.split("\n"):
537	        stripped = line.strip()
538	        if not stripped:
539	            continue
540	        stripped = re.sub(r"^#+\s*", "", stripped)
541	        stripped = re.sub(r"^[*_]+|[*_]+$", "", stripped).strip()
542	        if stripped:
543	            return stripped[:TITLE_MAX_CHARS]
544	    return None
545	
546	
547	def git_last_modified(repo, rel_dir):
548	    """Map repo-relative topic path -> last commit ISO date, in ONE git call.
549	
550	    Per-file `git log` would be one subprocess per topic (~175 today). The log
551	    walks newest-first, so the first date seen for a path is its last change.
552	
553	    Returns (dates, error). `error` is non-None only when git could not be run —
554	    the caller must not report committed topics as "undated" in that case, which
555	    would explain a broken toolchain as missing commits.
556	    """
557	    dates = {}
558	    rc, out, err = git(
559	        repo,
560	        ["log", "--format=%x1e%cI", "--name-only", "--", rel_dir],
561	        timeout=GIT_TIMEOUT_SEC,
562	    )
563	    if rc == GIT_UNRUNNABLE:
564	        return dates, "could not run git: %s" % (err.strip()[:200] or "unknown")
565	    if rc != 0:
566	        return dates, None
567	    current = None
568	    for line in out.split("\n"):
569	        if line.startswith("\x1e"):
570	            current = line[1:].strip()
571	            continue
572	        name = line.strip()
573	        if name and current and name not in dates:
574	            dates[name] = current
575	    return dates, None
576	
577	
578	def _age_days(iso_date):
579	    if not iso_date:
580	        return None
581	    try:
582	        # %cI is strict ISO-8601 with a numeric offset (e.g. +02:00). Python
583	        # 3.9's fromisoformat does not accept the colon in the offset on all
584	        # builds, so normalize before parsing.
585	        import datetime
586	        normalized = re.sub(r"([+-]\d{2}):(\d{2})$", r"\1\2", iso_date)
587	        parsed = datetime.datetime.strptime(normalized, "%Y-%m-%dT%H:%M:%S%z")
588	        delta = datetime.datetime.now(datetime.timezone.utc) - parsed
589	        return max(0, int(delta.total_seconds() // 86400))
590	    except (ValueError, ImportError):
591	        return None
592	
593	
594	def scan_lore(agent_dir, stale_days=DEFAULT_STALE_DAYS):
595	    lore_dir = os.path.join(agent_dir, "lore")
596	    if not os.path.isdir(lore_dir):
597	        return None, "no lore/ directory at %s" % lore_dir
598	
599	    try:
600	        names = sorted(
601	            n for n in os.listdir(lore_dir)
602	            if n.endswith(".md") and not n.startswith(".")
603	            # A directory named `something.md` is not a topic. Without this the
604	            # entry survives as a title-less, plausible-looking row.
605	            and os.path.isfile(os.path.join(lore_dir, n)))
606	    except (IOError, OSError) as exc:
607	        return None, str(exc)
608	
609	    repo = _repo_root_for(agent_dir)
610	    dates, git_error = {}, None
611	    if repo:
612	        rel_dir = os.path.relpath(lore_dir, repo)
613	        dates, git_error = git_last_modified(repo, rel_dir)
614	
615	    topics, undated, stale_count = [], 0, 0
616	    for name in names:
617	        path = os.path.join(lore_dir, name)
618	        rel = os.path.relpath(path, repo) if repo else name
619	        iso = dates.get(rel)
620	        age = _age_days(iso)
621	        if iso is None:
622	            undated += 1
623	        is_stale = age is not None and age > stale_days
624	        if is_stale:
625	            stale_count += 1
626	        try:
627	            size = os.path.getsize(path)
628	        except (IOError, OSError):
629	            size = None
630	        topics.append({
631	            "file": name,
632	            "title": topic_title(path),
633	            "last_modified": iso,
634	            "age_days": age,
635	            "stale": is_stale,
636	            "bytes": size,
637	        })
638	
639	    return {
640	        "lore_dir": lore_dir,
641	        "git_error": git_error,
642	        "count": len(topics),
643	        "stale_days_threshold": stale_days,
644	        "stale_count": stale_count,
645	        "undated_count": undated,
646	        "topics": topics,
647	    }, None
648	
649	
650	def _repo_root_for(agent_dir):
651	    """agents/<name>/ -> repo root, verified by lore-repo.md."""
652	    candidate = os.path.dirname(os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(agent_dir)))
653	    if os.path.isfile(os.path.join(candidate, "lore-repo.md")):
654	        return candidate
655	    return None
656	
657	
658	# --------------------------------------------------------------------------
659	# Subcommands
660	# --------------------------------------------------------------------------
661	
662	def cmd_discover(args, res):
663	    workspace = os.path.abspath(args.workspace)
664	    if not os.path.isdir(workspace):
665	        res.fail("workspace not found: %s" % workspace)
666	        res.data = {"workspace": workspace, "repos": [], "agents": []}
667	        return res
668	
669	    repos, agents = discover_workspace(workspace)
670	    res.data = {
671	        "workspace": workspace,
672	        "repos": repos,
673	        "agents": [{"name": a["name"], "repo": a["repo"], "dir": a["dir"],
674	                    "description": a["description"]} for a in agents],
675	    }
676	    if not repos:
677	        res.fail("no lore agent repos found in %s "
678	                 "(no top-level directory contains lore-repo.md)" % workspace)
679	    return res
680	
681	
682	def _resolve_agent(args, res, workspace):
683	    """Locate the target agent. Returns an agent dict, or None with res populated."""
684	    if args.agent_dir:
685	        agent_dir = os.path.abspath(args.agent_dir)
686	        role = os.path.join(agent_dir, "role.md")
687	        if not os.path.isfile(role):
688	            res.fail("no role.md at %s — not an agent directory" % agent_dir)
689	            return None
690	        repo = _repo_root_for(agent_dir)
691	        if not repo:
692	            res.warn("agent dir %s is not two levels below a lore-repo.md; "
693	                     "repo-scoped steps (pull, version) will be skipped" % agent_dir)
694	        fm = parse_frontmatter(read_text(role))
695	        return {
696	            "name": os.path.basename(agent_dir),
697	            "dir": agent_dir,
698	            "repo": repo,
699	            "description": fm.get("description"),
700	            "has_lore_context": os.path.isfile(
701	                os.path.join(agent_dir, "lore-context.md")),
702	        }
703	
704	    _, agents = discover_workspace(workspace)
705	    matches = [a for a in agents if a["name"] == args.agent]
706	    if not matches:
707	        res.fail("agent '%s' not found in %s" % (args.agent, workspace))
708	        res.data["available_agents"] = [
709	            {"name": a["name"], "repo": a["repo"], "description": a["description"]}
710	            for a in agents
711	        ]
712	        return None
713	    if len(matches) > 1:
714	        res.warn("agent name '%s' is ambiguous across %d repos; using %s"
715	                 % (args.agent, len(matches), matches[0]["repo"]))
716	    return matches[0]
717	
718	
719	def cmd_preflight(args, res):
720	    workspace = os.path.abspath(args.workspace)
721	    root = resolve_framework_root(args.framework_root)
722	    fw_version = framework_version(root)
723	    if fw_version is None:
724	        res.warn("no readable VERSION at %s — version check will be inconclusive" % root)
725	
726	    res.data["framework_root"] = root
727	    res.data["workspace"] = workspace
728	
729	    agent = _resolve_agent(args, res, workspace)
730	    if agent is None:
731	        return res
732	
733	    role_file = os.path.join(agent["dir"], "role.md")
734	    lore_context_file = os.path.join(agent["dir"], "lore-context.md")
735	    if not agent["has_lore_context"]:
736	        res.warn("no lore-context.md at %s — agent has no compacted knowledge yet"
737	                 % agent["dir"])
738	
739	    res.data["agent"] = {
740	        "name": agent["name"],
741	        "dir": agent["dir"],
742	        "repo": agent["repo"],
743	        "description": agent["description"],
744	        "role_file": role_file,
745	        "lore_context_file": lore_context_file if agent["has_lore_context"] else None,
746	    }
747	    # The files the caller must read into context next. Reading them is the
748	    # caller's job: their content is judgment material, not script output.
749	    res.data["read_next"] = [p for p in (role_file, lore_context_file if
750	                                         agent["has_lore_context"] else None) if p]
751	
752	    if agent["repo"]:
753	        res.data["pull"] = pull_repo(
754	            agent["repo"], ttl=args.ttl, fresh=args.fresh, do_pull=not args.no_pull)
755	        if res.data["pull"]["status"] == "failed":
756	            res.warn("auto-pull failed for %s: %s — continue in degraded mode"
757	                     % (agent["repo"], res.data["pull"]["detail"]))
758	        repo_version = parse_frontmatter(
759	            read_text(os.path.join(agent["repo"], "lore-repo.md"))).get("version")
760	        res.data["version"] = compare_versions(repo_version, fw_version)
761	        if res.data["version"]["verdict"] in ("repo-behind", "repo-ahead", "differs"):
762	            res.warn("version skew: repo=%s framework=%s (%s) — see version-check.md"
763	                     % (repo_version, fw_version, res.data["version"]["verdict"]))
764	    else:
765	        res.data["pull"] = {"status": "skipped", "detail": "no enclosing lore repo"}
766	        res.data["version"] = {"verdict": "unknown", "repo": None,
767	                               "framework": fw_version,
768	                               "detail": "no enclosing lore repo"}
769	
770	    res.data["teammate"] = detect_teammate() if not args.no_teammate_check else {
771	        "verdict": "unknown", "detail": "check suppressed by --no-teammate-check",
772	        "ancestors": []}
773	    return res
774	
775	
776	def cmd_scan(args, res):
777	    if args.agent_dir:
778	        agent_dir = os.path.abspath(args.agent_dir)
779	    else:
780	        workspace = os.path.abspath(args.workspace)
781	        agent = _resolve_agent(args, res, workspace)
782	        if agent is None:
783	            return res
784	        agent_dir = agent["dir"]
785	
786	    if not os.path.isdir(agent_dir):
787	        res.fail("agent directory not found: %s" % agent_dir)
788	        return res
789	
790	    data, err = scan_lore(agent_dir, stale_days=args.stale_days)
791	    if err:
792	        res.fail(err)
793	        res.data["agent_dir"] = agent_dir
794	        return res
795	    data["agent_dir"] = agent_dir
796	    res.data = data
797	    if data.get("git_error"):
798	        # Dates are missing because git could not run, not because the topics
799	        # are new. Saying "uncommitted or untracked" here would be a confident
800	        # wrong explanation.
801	        res.warn("could not read git history, so no topic dates or staleness "
802	                 "flags are available: %s" % data["git_error"])
803	    elif data["undated_count"]:
804	        res.warn("%d topic(s) have no git date (uncommitted or untracked)"
805	                 % data["undated_count"])
806	    return res
807	
808	
809	# --------------------------------------------------------------------------
810	# CLI
811	# --------------------------------------------------------------------------
812	
813	def build_parser():
814	    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(
815	        prog="lr-core",
816	        description="Deterministic substrate for the mechanical steps of lore skills.")
817	    parser.add_argument("--framework-root", default=None,
818	                        help="Override framework root (default: this script's parent).")
819	    sub = parser.add_subparsers(dest="command")
820	
821	    p_disc = sub.add_parser("discover", help="Enumerate lore agent repos and agents.")
822	    p_disc.add_argument("--workspace", default=".", help="Workspace root (default: cwd).")
823	    p_disc.set_defaults(func=cmd_discover)
824
