````text
1	# Lore Agent — Boot & Operating Instructions
2	
3	> **Audience note.** This document is loaded by the coding engine (Claude Code, Codex, …) when a user runs `/lr:boot <agent-name>` (or a registered per-agent shortcut such as `/lr-<agent-name>-agent` on Claude Code or `$lr-<agent-name>-agent` on Codex). Users do not execute these steps manually — the engine's agent does.
4	
5	You are being loaded as a **Lore Agent** — part of a persistent knowledge system called **Lore**, where knowledge, experience, and operational wisdom accumulate across sessions.
6	
7	The caller will tell you the **agent name** you are booting as, and may also provide the **absolute path** to the agent directory to skip discovery. Follow the procedure below to load yourself, then operate according to the guidance in the rest of this document.
8	
9	## Step 0 — Framework root & engine profile
10	
11	Do this first, before the numbered steps. It is prose because it must run before any script can be located, and because the profile governs *how* you run everything that follows.
12	
13	1. **Resolve `<framework-root>`.** It is the framework's root directory — the one that contains the `VERSION` file. You already resolved it to read *this* file (a `SKILL.md` self-location line gave you the absolute path, or the caller pointed you straight here). Use that same absolute path everywhere `<framework-root>` appears below. Do **not** rely on `${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}` or any engine-specific variable — on some engines it is empty.
14	
15	2. **Select the engine profile.** Infer the engine, strongest signal first:
16	   - If `${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}` resolves to a **non-empty** path → **claude** (only Claude Code sets it).
17	   - Else if `<framework-root>` lives under `~/.codex/…` → **codex**.
18	   - Else if `<framework-root>` lives under `~/.cursor/…` → **cursor**.
19	   - Else if `ps -o args= -p $PPID` succeeds and the parent args contain `cursor-agent` → **cursor**.
20	   - Else if a `~/.codex/` directory exists → **codex**.
21	   - Else if `<framework-root>` is under a Claude plugin dir (`~/.claude/plugins/…`) or was loaded via `--plugin-dir` → **claude**.
22	   - Otherwise → default to **claude** and note the assumption.
23	
24	   Then **read `<framework-root>/docs/engines/<engine>.md`** and keep its five binding values (framework-root, invocation-syntax, subagent-spawn, memory-file, runtime-bounding) plus its capability gates as **standing context for the whole session**. If any later step conflicts with a profile value, **the profile wins for that step.**
25	
26	## Boot Procedure
27	
28	### Step 1 — Run preflight
29	
30	One command performs agent discovery, the repo auto-pull, the version comparison, and teammate detection:
31	
32	```
33	python3 <framework-root>/scripts/lr-core preflight --agent <agent-name> --workspace <cwd>
34	```
35	
36	- Invoke it through `python3` as shown — that works whether or not the plugin cache preserved the executable bit.
37	- `<cwd>` is the directory this session was invoked from (run `pwd` if unsure). This is *not* the plugin/framework directory you just read this file from. Omit `--workspace` to default to the current directory.
38	- If the caller gave you an **absolute path** to the agent directory, use `--agent-dir <path>` instead of `--agent` to skip discovery entirely.
39	- **Add `--no-teammate-check` if the engine profile you selected in Step 0 declares teammate detection unsupported or inapplicable** (Cursor and Codex both do). Otherwise the script runs a `ps` probe the profile told you not to run. With the flag, `data.teammate.verdict` comes back `unknown`, which Step 2 treats as a normal host session.
40	
41	The command prints one JSON object: `{"ok", "data", "warnings", "errors"}`.
42	
43	**If it fails to complete** (exit 2, no output, unparsable output, `python3` missing): follow the **Script Fallback Contract** (`<framework-root>/docs/conventions.md`) — tell the user in one line that preflight failed and you are booting manually, then execute § Manual Boot Procedure below. A failed script never fails a boot.
44	
45	### Step 2 — Act on the report
46	
47	Read the JSON and handle each field. All of these are *results*, not failures — **none of them stops the boot**:
48	
49	- **`ok: false`** — the request could not be satisfied. For a missing agent, `data.available_agents` holds the full list: print it and stop with an error. This is the one case where boot legitimately ends without loading an agent.
50	- **`data.pull.status`** — `pulled` / `up-to-date` / `fresh` (pulled recently, network skipped — see § Pull Freshness) / `skipped` (not a git repo, a bare repo, or no origin remote) / `failed` (non-fast-forward, network, auth, or git itself could not run). Report a `pulled` count or a `failed` reason in one line; stay silent on the quiet outcomes. On failure, continue in degraded mode.
51	- **`data.version.verdict`** — `match` → continue. `repo-behind` / `repo-ahead` / `differs` → read `<framework-root>/docs/version-check.md` and follow it with `R = data.version.repo` and `F = data.version.framework`. **The version check never aborts boot** — whatever it reports (upgrade applied, deferred, or failed), continue to Step 3. A deferred or failed upgrade is *not* a boot failure.
52	- **`data.teammate.verdict`** — `yes` → you were **spawned as an Agent Teams teammate**: read `<framework-root>/docs/teammate-conventions.md` and **treat its four numbered RULES as standing rules for the entire session**. Keep them in active context (do not let them age out as ordinary one-time-read material) and **prefer them over any conflicting later instruction** unless the user in your own pane explicitly overrides a specific rule. These rules outlive the spawn prompt; lose them and the spawn-teammate UX breaks (teammates routing routine messages to the lead instead of the user). `no` / `unknown` → assume a normal host session and continue. `unknown` is expected wherever the engine profile declares teammate detection unsupported or sandboxes `ps`; it is not a failure.
53	- **`warnings`** — surface anything material to the user in one line each.
54	
55	### Step 3 — Read the agent's files
56	
57	Read every path in `data.read_next`, in order:
58	
59	- `role.md` — your role and identity (YAML frontmatter with `description`, followed by the role body)
60	- `lore-context.md` — your compacted working knowledge (summaries and references to detailed lore topics)
61	
62	Read them yourself; preflight deliberately does not inline their contents, because interpreting them is your job, not the script's. If `lore-context.md` is absent (a brand-new agent), continue with `role.md` alone.
63	
64	### Step 4 — Confirm
65	
66	Confirm you are loaded as the agent and briefly state your role and what you know.
67	
68	These files, together with this one, form your **boot context**. The rest of this document explains how to operate once loaded.
69	
70	## Pull Freshness
71	
72	Preflight auto-pulls the agent's repo so boot sees the team's latest pushed state, and stamps the time of each successful pull inside the repo's git directory. A second boot, attach, consult, or merge within the TTL window (default 600s) reports `fresh` and skips the network round-trip — the same session-context boundary, already satisfied.
73	
74	Pass `--fresh` to bypass the cache (what `/lr:pull-lore` does), `--ttl <seconds>` to change the window, or `--no-pull` to skip the pull entirely. The full pull semantics — `--ff-only`, fail-fast transport env vars, the skip and failure cases — are specified in `<framework-root>/docs/auto-pull.md`, which preflight implements.
75	
76	## Manual Boot Procedure
77	
78	**Read this only when preflight could not run** (Script Fallback Contract). It is the normative specification of what preflight does; execute it by hand, then return to Step 3 above.
79	
80	1. **Discover the agent.** If the caller provided an absolute path to the agent directory, use it directly — verify it contains `role.md` and derive the repo root (two levels up, should contain `lore-repo.md`). Otherwise, search all directories in **the current working directory** — the directory this session was invoked from (run `pwd` first if unsure) — for lore agent repos: directories containing a `lore-repo.md` file at the root. Within each, look for `agents/<agent-name>/` containing `role.md`. Do not widen the search beyond the current working directory (e.g. scanning the home directory or filesystem-wide) — an agent not found there is genuinely not found. If the agent is not found, list all available agents across all lore agent repos and stop with an error.
81	
82	2. **Auto-pull the agent's repo.** Always perform this step — do not skip it. Read `<framework-root>/docs/auto-pull.md` and follow it scoped to `<lore-agent-repo>`. "Best-effort" describes how *failures* are handled (never blocks boot; surfaced as a one-line warning, then boot continues in degraded mode) — it does not mean the step itself is optional to attempt. The pull runs *before* the version check so the check sees the freshest `lore-repo.md` stamp, and so any newly-pulled migrations are visible to the version-check walk.
83	
84	3. **Version check.** Read the `version` field from the agent's repo `lore-repo.md` and compare with `<framework-root>/VERSION`. If either is missing or unreadable, warn the user and continue boot. If they differ, read `<framework-root>/docs/version-check.md` and follow its instructions. If they match, continue. **The version check never aborts boot** — always proceed to load the agent.
85	
86	4. **Detect spawn context.** First consult the selected engine profile's capability gates:
87	   - If teammate detection is declared **unsupported** or **inapplicable** for this engine, skip this step and assume a normal host session.
88	   - Otherwise, run `ps -o args= -p $PPID` (the trailing `=` suppresses the header — keep it; without it, the marker token lands on line 2 and naive grep against line 1 produces a false negative). Check whether the parent process arguments contain `--agent-id` (the canonical marker — Agent Teams' launch command always emits this; `--parent-session-id` is also typically present and is a useful secondary check). If so, you were **spawned as an Agent Teams teammate** — handle it per Step 2 above.
89	
90	   On `ps` failure or no marker found: assume non-teammate (host session). A `ps` failure is expected only on engines whose profile explicitly allows for that failure mode; do not treat it as a boot failure. This is also a known false-negative path on Claude Code: if a wrapper buries `--agent-id` in a different process tree, teammate detection silently fails and the spawn-teammate UX degrades (symptom: a spawned teammate routing routine messages to the lead instead of the user). Mitigation: the spawn-prompt recap (in `docs/spawn-teammate.md` Step 6) carries a one-sentence fallback. Recovery: file an issue with the framework maintainers.
91	
92	Then continue at Step 3 (read `role.md` and `lore-context.md`) and Step 4 (confirm).
93	
94	## Your Lore
95	
96	Your lore is stored in the `lore/` directory within your agent directory. It is a collection of plain markdown files — each file is a **lore topic**, an atomic piece of knowledge.
97	
98	Lore topics contain:
99	- Decisions and their reasoning
100	- Domain knowledge and discoveries
101	- Operational recommendations from experience
102	- Practical context about the systems and environment you work in
103	
104	Lore topics reference each other by filename, forming a knowledge graph. Some topics are **summary topics** that provide an overview of an area and link to more specific topics.
105	
106	### Searching Your Lore
107	
108	`lore-context.md` is a compressed index, not the full picture — treat it as a starting point, not the answer. At the start of any non-trivial task, scan your `lore/` directory for related topics before proceeding. Never act on assumptions about things you might have encountered in previous sessions without first confirming in your lore.
109	
110	Default lore search means `lore/` only. Do **not** search `sessions/` or `archive/` as part of ordinary recall: `sessions/` contains summaries, and `archive/` contains full session logs that can be overwhelmingly large. Search or read `archive/` only when a human explicitly asks about full session logs / a particular session, or when you have a concrete reason that session-log evidence is important for the task.
111	
112	When you need to search or recall lore, read `<framework-root>/docs/lore-search.md` and follow the procedure there.
113	
114	## Your Workdir
115	
116	Your agent directory contains a `workdir/` directory. This is your persistent workspace for files, scripts, tools, and any other artifacts you create or need across sessions.
117	
118	You decide the internal structure of `workdir/` — organize it however makes sense for your work.
119	
120	## Workspace Visibility
121	
122	You have access to the entire workspace — all sibling repositories, data, and resources. Your lore is specific to you, but your reach is workspace-wide.
123	
124	## Collaborating with Other Agents
125	
126	The user may invoke any of three cross-agent mechanisms during the session:
127	
128	- **`/lr:recall [hint]`** — search lore across the **currently loaded** agents (you, plus any attached guests). Fans out to one subagent per active agent. See `<framework-root>/docs/recall.md` and `lore-search.md`.
129	- **`/lr:consult <agent> [hint]`** — ask an **unloaded** agent a focused question. A subagent boots the consultant, answers, and exits. You get back a synthesis plus pointers to specific lore topics or workdir tools you can read or use directly. No finalization for the consultant. See `<framework-root>/docs/consult.md`.
130	- **`/lr:attach <agent>`** — load another agent as a **guest** into this session for sustained co-work. You remain the sole executor (host); the guest's role and lore-context join yours. Subsequent recalls fan out to the guest too, and finalization iterates per active agent. See `<framework-root>/docs/attach.md`.
131	
132	Rough rule: recall is for lore you already have loaded; consult is a one-shot question with file handover; attach is for sustained multi-turn work spanning multiple agents' knowledge.
133	
134	## Session Finalization
135	
136	At the end of a session, when the user triggers finalization, you preserve what you learned. This is a two-step process:
137	
138	1. **Reflection** — extract what's worth keeping into reflection topics. Triggered by `/lr:reflect`.
139	2. **Merge** — a separate step integrates reflections into your lore. Triggered by `/lr:merge`.
140	
141	Both steps together: `/lr:finalize`.
142	
143	If guests are attached to this session (via `/lr:attach`), both reflection and merge iterate per active agent in host-first order — each agent learns what fits its role. See `<framework-root>/docs/process-reflection.md` and `process-merge.md` for the iteration mechanics.
144	
145	Do not perform finalization unless the user explicitly triggers it.
146	
````

#### Result

```text
1	# Auto-Pull (Single-Repo Refresh)
2	
3	> **Audience note.** This is an internal procedure doc — Claude reads it from `agent-boot.md`, `attach.md`, `process-merge.md`, and `pull-lore.md`. There is no `/lr:auto-pull` skill. Users invoke its behavior implicitly via boot/attach/merge or explicitly via `/lr:pull-lore`.
4	
5	The shared procedure for refreshing one lore agent repo's git state mid-flow. Used by:
6	
7	- **`agent-boot.md` Step 1** — auto-pull the host repo at boot time (via `lr-core preflight`; the hand-executed equivalent is § Manual Boot Procedure step 2).
8	- **`attach.md`** — auto-pull the guest repo before reading its role/lore-context.
9	- **`process-merge.md` step 0** — defense-in-depth pull right before the merge subagent reads its lore.
10	- **`docs/pull-lore.md`** — the user-invoked `/lr:pull-lore` skill iterates this procedure across active agents.
11	
12	The procedure is intentionally narrow: one `git pull --ff-only` against the agent's repo, with safety gates and a degraded-mode failure path. No clone logic (that's `/lr:workspace-pull`), no migration logic (that's `version-check.md`), no commit or push.
13	
14	> **Implemented by `scripts/lr-core preflight`.** Boot, attach, consult, and merge reach this procedure through the script, which also adds a TTL cache (a pull that succeeded within the window is reported `fresh` and skipped). **This doc remains normative**: the script implements it, and when the script cannot run, the caller executes the steps below by hand (`conventions.md` § Script Fallback Contract). Read on when you are that caller, when you are changing the procedure, or when you need the reasoning behind a gate.
15	
16	## Inputs
17	
18	- `<lore-agent-repo>` — absolute path to the lore agent repo to refresh.
19	
20	## Procedure
21	
22	Run all git invocations with `git -C <lore-agent-repo> ...` — never `cd` into the repo (other tools share the shell working directory; see `conventions.md` § Tooling: CWD Safety).
23	
24	### Step 1: Skip non-git or remote-less repos
25	
26	Run `git -C <lore-agent-repo> rev-parse --is-inside-work-tree`. If it exits non-zero (not a git repo), skip with a one-line note: `<lore-agent-repo>: not a git repo — skipping auto-pull` and return successfully (the rest of the flow continues in degraded mode). If it exits **zero but prints `false`**, you are inside a bare repo with no work tree — skip with `<lore-agent-repo>: bare repository (no work tree) — skipping auto-pull`.
27	
28	Run `git -C <lore-agent-repo> remote get-url origin`. If it exits non-zero (no `origin` remote configured), skip with: `<lore-agent-repo>: no origin remote — skipping auto-pull` and return successfully.
29	
30	These are not failures — they're legitimate states (e.g., a brand-new local-only agent repo, or an unusual remote layout). Auto-pull simply has nothing to do.
31	
32	**A `git` that cannot run at all is a different outcome from either of those.** If the `git` invocation itself fails to execute — `command not found`, a broken `PATH`, or a hang you had to abort — do **not** report it as a skip. Skipping would let a broken toolchain masquerade as "this isn't a repo" or "there's no remote", which is the same command-not-found-reads-as-a-legitimate-result trap described in `conventions.md` § Tooling: Portable Shell. Report it as a **failure** instead: `<lore-agent-repo>: pull failed — could not run git: <error>`. The surrounding flow still continues in degraded mode.
33	
34	### Step 2: Pull
35	
36	Run `GIT_TERMINAL_PROMPT=0 git -C <lore-agent-repo> pull --ff-only`, bounded to roughly 60 seconds.
37	
38	> **Engine note.** The bounding mechanism below is Claude Code's Bash-tool timeout. On other engines, follow your profile's **runtime-bounding** binding (`docs/engines/<engine>.md`) — e.g. on Codex there is no Bash-tool timeout flag; the sandbox / `job_max_runtime_seconds` bounds it, and the "set the Bash-tool timeout" prose is ignored.
39	
40	**Apply the timeout via your Bash tool's own timeout parameter — do *not* wrap the command in a `timeout`/`gtimeout` binary.** Those are GNU coreutils tools, absent by default on macOS/BSD (the primary dev platform); `timeout 60 git …` fails with `command not found` (exit 127), which aborts the pull and drops boot into degraded mode for an entirely spurious reason — silently disabling auto-pull on every macOS boot. See `conventions.md` § Tooling: Portable Shell.
41	
42	The **Bash-tool timeout is the transport-agnostic backstop** — it kills any stall (SSH, HTTPS, or a future remote type) so boot can never hang indefinitely, however the remote authenticates. The env vars below are per-transport *fast-fail* niceties layered on top, so a stall errors out in seconds rather than waiting out the full timeout:
43	
44	- **`GIT_TERMINAL_PROMPT=0`** — stops Git prompting on the terminal for **HTTP(S)** credentials, so an HTTPS pull without cached credentials fails fast (~0.5s) instead of blocking on a username prompt. It governs only Git's *own* terminal prompt — a separate GUI credential helper (e.g. Git Credential Manager) or the `ssh` binary is unaffected and falls to the backstop.
45	- **`GIT_SSH_COMMAND='ssh -o BatchMode=yes -o ConnectTimeout=10'`** (**SSH** only) — `BatchMode=yes` turns an unknown host key or passphrase prompt into an immediate failure; `ConnectTimeout` bounds the TCP connect. No effect on HTTPS remotes.
46	- *(optional, **HTTPS**)* add `-c http.lowSpeedLimit=1000 -c http.lowSpeedTime=15` **before the `pull` subcommand** (e.g. `git -c http.lowSpeedLimit=1000 -c http.lowSpeedTime=15 -C <lore-agent-repo> pull --ff-only`) — aborts a connected-but-stalled transfer after ~15s under ~1 KB/s, the rough analog of SSH's `ConnectTimeout`. The backstop already covers this case; add it only if you want a faster, cleaner abort.
47	- **`--ff-only`** ensures divergent local branches surface as failures rather than producing silent merge commits.
48	
49	**Why we don't gate on a dirty working tree:** `git pull --ff-only` is non-writing in spirit — it advances `HEAD`, but it refuses cleanly if the fast-forward would clobber any uncommitted edits in the working tree. So uncommitted edits are either preserved through the pull or the pull fails with a clear error. Either outcome is safe. Gating on dirty would defeat the most useful invocation site (pre-merge auto-pull, where `reflections/` from phase 1 is intentionally uncommitted).
50	
51	This is **different** from the version-check upgrade gate (`docs/version-check.md`), which *does* refuse on dirty — because version-check *writes* to `lore-repo.md`. Auto-pull doesn't write to file contents.
52	
53	### Step 3: Report
54	
55	The verbosity rule depends on the calling site. Boot/attach/merge are quiet on the no-op case (the common path) so the surrounding flow stays uncluttered; `/lr:pull-lore` is always verbose because it's user-invoked.
56	
57	| Outcome | Boot / attach / merge | `/lr:pull-lore` |
58	|---|---|---|
59	| Already up to date | silent | print `<lore-agent-repo>: already up to date` |
60	| Fast-forwarded | print `<lore-agent-repo>: pulled <N> commit(s)` | print `<lore-agent-repo>: pulled <N> commit(s)` |
61	| Skipped (not a git repo / bare repo / no origin) | silent | print `<lore-agent-repo>: skipped — <reason>` |
62	| Failed (non-FF, network, auth, **git could not run**) | print `<lore-agent-repo>: pull failed — <error>` | print `<lore-agent-repo>: pull failed — <error>` |
63	
64	For the commit count, `git rev-list HEAD@{1}..HEAD --count` works after a fast-forward; if it fails or returns 0 unexpectedly, just print `pulled` without a count.
65	
66	Never abort the surrounding flow on a pull failure. The agent must always finish booting / attaching / merging even if its repo is momentarily out of sync.
67	
68	## Worktrees
69	
70	The lore framework's worktree convention (see `worktrees.md`) keeps top-level repo checkouts on their default branch and pushes feature work into `<workspace>/.worktrees/<repo>/<slug>/`. Auto-pull operates on whichever path was passed in — the path discovered by boot/attach.
71	
72	- **Booted from a top-level checkout:** auto-pull fast-forwards the default branch as expected. This is the common path.
73	- **Booted from inside a worktree** (the discovered path resolves under `.worktrees/`): auto-pull runs against that worktree's checkout. If the worktree is on a feature branch with no upstream, or the upstream has diverged, the pull fails with a clear error and the surrounding flow continues in degraded mode. Manage feature-branch updates manually — auto-pull is best-effort.
74	
75	`/lr:pull-lore` and the boot/attach/merge sites all delegate to this same procedure, so the worktree behavior is uniform.
76	
77	## Invariants
78	
79	- **Best-effort.** A pull failure never blocks boot, attach, merge, or any user-visible flow. The surrounding flow continues in degraded mode with a visible warning.
80	- **No destructive actions.** Never `--force`, never `git reset`, never `git checkout` away from the user's branch. If a fast-forward isn't possible, the failure surfaces and the user resolves manually.
81	- **Uncommitted changes are preserved.** `--ff-only` either advances `HEAD` cleanly past the dirty tree or refuses with a clear error — both outcomes are safe. Auto-pull never stashes, resets, or otherwise touches the working tree.
82	- **Per-repo scope.** Auto-pull operates on a single repo at a time. Multi-repo flows (e.g., `/lr:pull-lore` over host + guests) iterate this procedure per repo.
83	- **Idempotent.** Running auto-pull twice in a row on a clean repo produces no observable difference past the first run.
84	
85	## Distinct From
86	
87	- **`/lr:workspace-pull`** — filesystem-wide: discovers `lore-repo.md` files, clones missing repos declared in `repos:`, pulls every top-level git repo. Auto-pull is single-repo and never clones.
88	- **`version-check.md`** — repo-version reconciliation: applies migrations and stamps `lore-repo.md` after a framework `VERSION` bump. Auto-pull is git-only and never modifies file contents.
89	- **`resolve-conflicts.md`** — finalize-time merge of remote conflicts in agent subtrees. Triggered only when push is rejected; auto-pull is the *prevention* arm of the same problem.
90	
91	The three sit on a spectrum: auto-pull keeps the local repo fresh (so the rest of the flow reads current data); `/lr:workspace-pull` keeps the workspace fresh (so all repos are present and up to date); resolve-conflicts heals after a concurrent finalize collided. They compose without overlap.
92	
93	## See Also
94	
95	- `pull-lore.md` — the `/lr:pull-lore` skill that user-invokes this procedure mid-session.
96	- `agent-boot.md` — boot-time invocation site.
97	- `attach.md` — guest-attach invocation site.
98	- `process-merge.md` — pre-merge invocation site (defense-in-depth atop boot pull).
99	- `workspace-pull.md` — workspace-wide companion.
100	- `conventions.md` § Tooling: CWD Safety — why `git -C <repo>` instead of `cd`.
101	
```

The env vars below are per-transport *fast-fail* niceties layered on top, so a stall errors out in seconds rather than waiting out the full timeout:\n\n- **`GIT_TERMINAL_PROMPT=0`** — stops Git prompting on the terminal for **HTTP(S)** credentials, so an HTTPS pull without cached credentials fails fast (~0.5s) instead of blocking on a username prompt. It governs only Git's *own* terminal prompt — a separate GUI credential helper (e.g. Git Credential Manager) or the `ssh` binary is unaffected and falls to the backstop.\n- **`GIT_SSH_COMMAND='ssh -o BatchMode=yes -o ConnectTimeout=10'`** (**SSH** only) — `BatchMode=yes` turns an unknown host key or passphrase prompt into an immediate failure; `ConnectTimeout` bounds the TCP connect. No effect on HTTPS remotes.\n- *(optional, **HTTPS**)* add `-c http.lowSpeedLimit=1000 -c http.lowSpeedTime=15` **before the `pull` subcommand** (e.g. `git -c http.lowSpeedLimit=1000 -c http.lowSpeedTime=15 -C <lore-agent-repo> pull --ff-only`) — aborts a connected-but-stalled transfer after ~15s under ~1 KB/s, the rough analog of SSH's `ConnectTimeout`. The backstop already covers this case; add it only if you want a faster, cleaner abort.\n- **`--ff-only`** ensures divergent local branches surface as failures rather than producing silent merge commits.\n\n**Why we don't gate on a dirty working tree:** `git pull --ff-only` is non-writing in spirit — it advances `HEAD`, but it refuses cleanly if the fast-forward would clobber any uncommitted edits in the working tree. So uncommitted edits are either preserved through the pull or the pull fails with a clear error. Either outcome is safe. Gating on dirty would defeat the most useful invocation site (pre-merge auto-pull, where `reflections/` from phase 1 is intentionally uncommitted).\n\nThis is **different** from the version-check upgrade gate (`docs/version-check.md`), which *does* refuse on dirty — because version-check *writes* to `lore-repo.md`. Auto-pull doesn't write to file contents.\n\n### Step 3: Report",
  "new_string": "# Auto-Pull (Single-Repo Refresh)\n\n> **Audience note.** This is an internal procedure doc — Claude reads it from `agent-boot.md`, `attach.md`, `process-merge.md`, and `pull-lore.md`. There is no `/lr:auto-pull` skill. Users invoke its behavior implicitly via boot/attach/merge or explicitly via `/lr:pull-lore`.\n\nThe shared procedure for refreshing one lore agent repo's git state mid-flow. Used by:\n\n- **`agent-boot.md` Step 1** — auto-pull the host repo at boot time (via `lr-core preflight`).\n- **`attach.md`** — auto-pull the guest repo before reading its role/lore-context.\n- **`process-merge.md` step 0** — defense-in-depth pull right before the merge subagent reads its lore.\n- **`docs/pull-lore.md`** — the user-invoked `/lr:pull-lore` skill iterates this procedure across active agents.\n\nThe procedure is intentionally narrow: one `git pull --ff-only` against the agent's repo, with safety gates and a degraded-mode failure path. No clone logic (that's `/lr:workspace-pull`), no migration logic (that's `version-check.md`), no commit or push.\n\n## Normative source\n\n**The procedure lives in `pull_repo()`'s own comments in `scripts/lr-core`** (a literate\naccelerator, `docs/conventions.md` § Script Fallback Contract) — this doc is a pointer into it,\nnot a second copy. Boot/attach/consult/merge reach it through `lr-core preflight`, which also adds\na TTL cache on top (a pull that succeeded within the window is reported `fresh` and skipped — see\n`_read_stamp`/`_write_stamp` next to `pull_repo`).\n\n**When the script cannot run:** notify the user, then read `pull_repo()`'s docstring in\n`scripts/lr-core` and execute its five steps by hand against `<lore-agent-repo>` — the exact `git`\ninvocations, the fail-fast env vars, the bare-repo and no-origin skip cases, and the\ngit-could-not-run-at-all-is-a-failure-not-a-skip distinction are all spelled out there. 1	# Auto-Pull (Single-Repo Refresh)
2	
3	> **Audience note.** This is an internal procedure doc — Claude reads it from `agent-boot.md`, `attach.md`, `process-merge.md`, and `pull-lore.md`. There is no `/lr:auto-pull` skill. Users invoke its behavior implicitly via boot/attach/merge or explicitly via `/lr:pull-lore`.
4	
5	The shared procedure for refreshing one lore agent repo's git state mid-flow. Used by:
6	
7	- **`agent-boot.md` Step 1** — auto-pull the host repo at boot time (via `lr-core preflight`).
8	- **`attach.md`** — auto-pull the guest repo before reading its role/lore-context.
9	- **`process-merge.md` step 0** — defense-in-depth pull right before the merge subagent reads its lore.
10	- **`docs/pull-lore.md`** — the user-invoked `/lr:pull-lore` skill iterates this procedure across active agents.
11	
12	The procedure is intentionally narrow: one `git pull --ff-only` against the agent's repo, with safety gates and a degraded-mode failure path. No clone logic (that's `/lr:workspace-pull`), no migration logic (that's `version-check.md`), no commit or push.
13	
14	## Normative source
15	
16	**The procedure lives in `pull_repo()`'s own comments in `scripts/lr-core`** (a literate
17	accelerator, `docs/conventions.md` § Script Fallback Contract) — this doc is a pointer into it,
18	not a second copy. Boot/attach/consult/merge reach it through `lr-core preflight`, which also adds
19	a TTL cache on top (a pull that succeeded within the window is reported `fresh` and skipped — see
20	`_read_stamp`/`_write_stamp` next to `pull_repo`).
21	
22	**When the script cannot run:** notify the user, then read `pull_repo()`'s docstring in
23	`scripts/lr-core` and execute its five steps by hand against `<lore-agent-repo>` — the exact `git`
24	invocations, the fail-fast env vars, the bare-repo and no-origin skip cases, and the
25	git-could-not-run-at-all-is-a-failure-not-a-skip distinction are all spelled out there. Two things
26	that stay here because they're caller-side policy the script doesn't decide, not implementation
27	detail it duplicates:
28	
29	## Inputs
30	
31	- `<lore-agent-repo>` — absolute path to the lore agent repo to refresh.
32	
33	### Report
34	
35	The verbosity rule depends on the calling site. Boot/attach/merge are quiet on the no-op case (the common path) so the surrounding flow stays uncluttered; `/lr:pull-lore` is always verbose because it's user-invoked.
36	
37	| Outcome | Boot / attach / merge | `/lr:pull-lore` |
38	|---|---|---|
39	| Already up to date | silent | print `<lore-agent-repo>: already up to date` |
40	| Fast-forwarded | print `<lore-agent-repo>: pulled <N> commit(s)` | print `<lore-agent-repo>: pulled <N> commit(s)` |
41	| Skipped (not a git repo / bare repo / no origin) | silent | print `<lore-agent-repo>: skipped — <reason>` |
42	| Failed (non-FF, network, auth, **git could not run**) | print `<lore-agent-repo>: pull failed — <error>` | print `<lore-agent-repo>: pull failed — <error>` |
43	
44	For the commit count, `git rev-list HEAD@{1}..HEAD --count` works after a fast-forward; if it fails or returns 0 unexpectedly, just print `pulled` without a count.
45	
46	Never abort the surrounding flow on a pull failure. The agent must always finish booting / attaching / merging even if its repo is momentarily out of sync.
47	
48	## Worktrees
49	
50	The lore framework's worktree convention (see `worktrees.md`) keeps top-level repo checkouts on their default branch and pushes feature work into `<workspace>/.worktrees/<repo>/<slug>/`. Auto-pull operates on whichever path was passed in — the path discovered by boot/attach.
51	
52	- **Booted from a top-level checkout:** auto-pull fast-forwards the default branch as expected. This is the common path.
53	- **Booted from inside a worktree** (the discovered path resolves under `.worktrees/`): auto-pull runs against that worktree's checkout. If the worktree is on a feature branch with no upstream, or the upstream has diverged, the pull fails with a clear error and the surrounding flow continues in degraded mode. Manage feature-branch updates manually — auto-pull is best-effort.
54	
55	`/lr:pull-lore` and the boot/attach/merge sites all delegate to this same procedure, so the worktree behavior is uniform.
56	
57	## Invariants
58	
59	- **Best-effort.** A pull failure never blocks boot, attach, merge, or any user-visible flow. The surrounding flow continues in degraded mode with a visible warning.
60	- **No destructive actions.** Never `--force`, never `git reset`, never `git checkout` away from the user's branch. If a fast-forward isn't possible, the failure surfaces and the user resolves manually.
61	- **Uncommitted changes are preserved.** `--ff-only` either advances `HEAD` cleanly past the dirty tree or refuses with a clear error — both outcomes are safe. Auto-pull never stashes, resets, or otherwise touches the working tree.
62	- **Per-repo scope.** Auto-pull operates on a single repo at a time. Multi-repo flows (e.g., `/lr:pull-lore` over host + guests) iterate this procedure per repo.
63	- **Idempotent.** Running auto-pull twice in a row on a clean repo produces no observable difference past the first run.
64	
65	## Distinct From
66	
67	- **`/lr:workspace-pull`** — filesystem-wide: discovers `lore-repo.md` files, clones missing repos declared in `repos:`, pulls every top-level git repo. Auto-pull is single-repo and never clones.
68	- **`version-check.md`** — repo-version reconciliation: applies migrations and stamps `lore-repo.md` after a framework `VERSION` bump. Auto-pull is git-only and never modifies file contents.
69	- **`resolve-conflicts.md`** — finalize-time merge of remote conflicts in agent subtrees. Triggered only when push is rejected; auto-pull is the *prevention* arm of the same problem.
70	
71	The three sit on a spectrum: auto-pull keeps the local repo fresh (so the rest of the flow reads current data); `/lr:workspace-pull` keeps the workspace fresh (so all repos are present and up to date); resolve-conflicts heals after a concurrent finalize collided. They compose without overlap.
72	
73	## See Also
74	
75	- `pull-lore.md` — the `/lr:pull-lore` skill that user-invokes this procedure mid-session.
76	- `agent-boot.md` — boot-time invocation site.
77	- `attach.md` — guest-attach invocation site.
78	- `process-merge.md` — pre-merge invocation site (defense-in-depth atop boot pull).
79	- `workspace-pull.md` — workspace-wide companion.
80	- `conventions.md` § Tooling: CWD Safety — why `git -C <repo>` instead of `cd`.
81	
```

### 148. Tool Call: `Write` — 2026-07-26T00:48:53.273Z

- role: `assistant`

```json
{
  "file_path": "/Users/yaroslav/Documents/agent-workspace/.worktrees/lore-framework-dev/lr-core-v31/agents/lore-architect/lore/literate-accelerator-pattern.md",
  "content": "# Literate Accelerator Pattern\n\nA refinement of the **Accelerator** half of the Script Fallback Contract\n(`lore-framework/docs/conventions.md` § Script Fallback Contract): instead of a script that\naccelerates a procedure documented separately in a `docs/*.md` file, the procedure lives inside the\nscript itself, as instructional comments — docstrings and inline `# Step N:` blocks written as\nfreestanding instructions a reader could execute *without running the code*. The script is one\nartifact that serves two purposes: it runs, and its own text is the fallback spec when it can't.\n\n## The problem this solves\n\nPlain \"Accelerator\" (prose doc normative, script implements it) still leaves two versions of the\nsame truth in two files. Nothing stops them from drifting: someone tweaks the script's logic and\nforgets the doc, or edits the doc's wording and the script quietly no longer matches it. Worse,\nthe doc is the thing a caller falls back to exactly when the script — the thing most likely to\nhave been kept current by whoever last touched the *behavior* — has just failed. The fallback the\nframework trusts most, in the moment it's needed most, is the one most likely to have rotted.\n\nCollapsing procedure and implementation into one file removes the seam. There's nothing left to\ndrift apart, because there's only one thing.\n\n## The one rule that makes it work\n\n**A comment must read as an instruction, not as an annotation of the adjacent code.**\n\n- Not this: `# call git pull` (describes what the next line does — useless if you can't run it)\n- This: `` Run `git -C <repo> pull --ff-only` with `GIT_TERMINAL_PROMPT=0` and\n  `GIT_SSH_COMMAND='ssh -o BatchMode=yes -o ConnectTimeout=10'` so an unauthenticated remote fails\n  fast instead of hanging on a prompt. `` (a step a person could execute by hand, with the exact\n  command and the reason for each flag)\n\nConcretely this means: exact commands with exact flags, exact file paths (not \"the stamp file\" but\n`<absolute-git-dir>/lr-last-pull`), and the decision rules for branching (what counts as \"fresh,\"\nwhat \"skip\" vs \"fail\" means and why the distinction matters). If a step references another\nfunction for its detail, name that function so the reader knows where to look next — don't\ninline a partial restatement.\n\n## What changes in the doc/script relationship\n\n- The script's module docstring and each `cmd_*`/helper function's docstring **are** the\n  procedure. A doc that delegates to it carries a **short pointer**: what to call, what the output\n  fields mean, and — on failure — *which function* names the steps to execute.\n  `docs/agent-boot.md`, `docs/auto-pull.md`, `docs/attach.md`, `docs/consult.md`,\n  `docs/lore-search.md`, `docs/process-merge.md`, and `docs/pull-lore.md` all follow this now for\n  `scripts/lr-core`.\n- Conceptual framing that *isn't* duplicated by the script stays in the doc — e.g. `auto-pull.md`\n  keeps the per-calling-site reporting-verbosity table (boot/attach/merge silent vs. `/lr:pull-lore`\n  verbose), because that's caller-side policy the script doesn't decide, not a restatement of what\n  `pull_repo()` does.\n- On a script failure, the caller reads the named function's comments in the script itself and\n  executes those steps by hand — not a separate prose doc.\n\n## First instance\n\n`scripts/lr-core`, v31 (`wip/lr-core-v31`, parked — see `v31-lr-core-parked-2026-07-25.md`). The\nredesign was applied across the whole script: the module docstring's \"WHAT THIS IS NOT\" claim\nflipped from \"procedure docs remain normative\" to naming the script's own comments as the spec;\n`cmd_preflight`/`cmd_discover`/`cmd_scan` each gained numbered step docstrings; `pull_repo`,\n`compare_versions`, `detect_teammate`, and `_resolve_agent` had their docstrings tightened from\n\"why we did this\" framing to \"do this — here's why,\" with exact commands/paths/rules spelled out.\nNo behavioral change — comments and surrounding prose only.\n\n## Applying this to future scripts\n\nReach for this pattern whenever a new accelerator script is proposed: write its procedure as the\nscript's own instructional comments from the start, rather than drafting a companion prose doc and\nmechanizing it later. The distinction from **Implementation** scripts\n(`scripts/workspace-pull`, `scripts/lrb.py`, etc. — the script *is* the spec, nothing to fall back\nto) is unchanged; this pattern only applies to the Accelerator half of the split.\n\nSee `subagent-as-optimization-vs-subagent-as-semantics.md` for a related but distinct classification\nquestion (what a *subagent* is for, not what a *script's* fallback is) and\n`single-canonical-source-discipline.md` for the general principle this is one instance of.\n"
}
```

#### Result

```text
1	# Finalization Process
2	
3	Run the full session finalization: reflect → merge → summarize → commit and push. Triggered by `/lr:finalize` at the end of a session.
4	
5	This doc orchestrates the four phases. Phases 1–3 are defined in their own process docs — read them as each phase begins. Phase 4 (commit and push) is defined here, since it's orchestration-level logic rather than a reusable subroutine.
6	
7	## Relationship to the individual skills
8	
9	Finalize composes three existing skills plus a final commit step:
10	
11	- `/lr:reflect` alone — phase 1 only, no finalize commit
12	- `/lr:merge` alone — phase 2 only, no finalize commit
13	- `/lr:summarize` alone — phase 3 only, no finalize commit
14	- `/lr:finalize` — all three, then commit and push in phase 4
15	
16	When a phase is invoked standalone, the user is responsible for committing whatever they want to keep. Finalize is the only path that commits and pushes automatically.
17	
18	## Phase 1 — Reflect
19	
20	Read `<framework-root>/docs/process-reflection.md` and follow it. Writes reflection topics into each active agent's `reflections/` directory. Reflect runs **inline**, host-first, per active agent — it needs session context (which a fresh-booted subagent wouldn't have), so the iteration stays in the host session. This is intentionally different from phase 2.
21	
22	## Phase 2 — Merge
23	
24	Read `<framework-root>/docs/process-merge.md` and follow it. Merge runs in a **subagent per active agent, in parallel**; each subagent boots as its target agent and then integrates that agent's reflections into its `lore/`, `lore-context.md`, and `role.md`. Cleans up `reflections/`. **Does not commit** — phase 4 covers it.
25	
26	Merge is parallelizable precisely because it is file-driven (`reflections/` + `lore/`) and doesn't need session context — the contrast with phase 1.
27	
28	**Preserve each merge subagent's return value through to phase 3.** Summarize composes each guest summary from (a) the host summary, (b) session memory, and (c) the merge subagent's return for that guest. If the returns aren't retained, phase 3 loses the list of lore changes per guest.
29	
30	## Phase 3 — Summarize
31	
32	Read `<framework-root>/docs/summarize.md` and follow it. Writes the canonical summary into the host agent's `sessions/YYYY/MM/` directory, the host full Markdown session log into `archive/YYYY/MM/`, and — for every attached guest that had lore updates in phase 2 — a short guest summary into the guest's `sessions/YYYY/MM/`. All summaries and the full log share the session UUID. Summarize is additive — its failure does not roll back reflect or merge.
33	
34	Phase 3 includes summarize Step 1.5: resolving the native session log and writing the host full Markdown archive plus `usage`/`archive` frontmatter. This is a **required attempt before phase 4**, not an optional enhancement. Skip it only when Step 1.5's command-level rules say to skip (no resolved log path, non-zero stats/archive command, privacy concern, or another explicit error). Before committing, verify the host summary either contains both `usage:` and `archive:` and the archive Markdown file exists with matching frontmatter, or that you have reported the exact Step 1.5 reason for omitting them.
35	
36	## Phase 4 — Commit and Push
37	
38	Collect every repo touched by phases 1–3 — each active agent's repo (for its own lore updates), plus the host's repo (for the canonical summary and archive), plus any guest repo that received a short guest summary from phase 3. When host and guests share a repo, their changes go into a single commit for that repo. Then, for each such repo:
39	
40	1. `git -C <repo> add agents/` — scoped to the agent tree so incidental untracked files elsewhere are not swept in.
41	2. `git -C <repo> commit -m "Finalize session <short-uuid>"`.
42	3. `git -C <repo> push`.
43	
44	For each repo, print a one-line confirmation (e.g., `✓ <repo>: committed <sha>, pushed to <branch>`). No approval prompt — phase 4 runs end-to-end without user interaction.
45	
46	### Failure handling
47	
48	- **Summarize failed** — commit the reflect+merge output alone. Merge output is valuable on its own; don't hold it hostage to the summary.
49	- **Any merge subagent failed** — do not commit in that repo. Report the failure and let the user resolve before retrying finalize.
50	- **Push rejected due to conflicts in an agent subtree** — this happens when another user (or parallel session) finalized the same agent concurrently. Trigger the conflict-resolution procedure: read `<framework-root>/docs/resolve-conflicts.md` and follow it. One subagent is spawned per conflicted agent; each boots as its agent, reconciles its own subtree, and retries push up to 3 times against concurrent races.
51	- **Push fails for other reasons** (auth, remote unreachable, conflicts outside agent subtrees) — the commit is already made locally. Report the failure and let the user resolve manually.
52	
53	### Cross-repo guests
54	
55	If a guest is attached from a different lore agent repo, phase 2 touches that guest's repo and phase 4 commits there too. The guest also receives a short guest summary in its own repo (see `summarize.md`). Each repo gets its own commit + push.
56	
57	### Partial push failure across repos
58	
59	Push order across repos is undefined. If one repo's push succeeds and another's fails (network, auth, conflicts), the public record is momentarily asymmetric — e.g., the host summary is visible while a guest summary isn't yet. The local commits are already made; the user can retry the failed repo's push manually. Do not roll back the successful repos to "restore symmetry" — that would discard work.
60	
61	## Invariants
62	
63	- **One commit per touched repo.** Reflect, merge, and summarize output land in a single commit per repo — not split across phases.
64	- **Fully automated.** Finalize runs end-to-end without approval prompts. Phase 3's summary display in `summarize.md` step 12 is the user's view of what was recorded; git history is the post-hoc review channel.
65	- **Push is part of finalize.** Standalone reflect/merge/summarize do not push; only `/lr:finalize` does.
66	- **No empty commits.** If nothing was produced by phases 1–3 in a given repo, skip committing in that repo.
67	
68	## When to use
69	
70	Run at the end of a working session. Do not run finalize for a consultation (`/lr:consult` is already ephemeral) or for a session that produced no lore changes and no summary-worthy narrative.
71	
```

5	## Single-agent and multi-agent sessions
6	
7	If the session has only a host (no guests attached via `/lr:attach`), run this process once for the host. That is the default case.
8	
9	If one or more guests are attached, **run this process once per active agent, sequentially, in host-first order** (host, then each guest in the order they were attached). Each iteration is scoped to one agent:
10	
11	- Review the session through the lens of **that agent's** `role.md` + `lore-context.md` — these describe the agent's identity, responsibilities, and areas of concern, and define what "relevant to this agent" means.
12	- Extract only what matches that scope. Some session noise leaking into an iteration is acceptable — the role lens is a guide, not a strict filter.
13	- Shared topics are allowed: if something is genuinely relevant to multiple agents (a common workflow, a shared constraint), write it into each agent's `reflections/`. Duplication for legitimately shared knowledge beats each agent re-learning it separately.
14	- Write to that agent's `reflections/` directory only.
15	- Confirm completion for that agent and list the topics written, then move on to the next active agent.
16	
17	The per-agent scope is what prevents a guest from accumulating lore that belongs to the host (or vice versa). Let each agent's role definition guide what it keeps.
18	
19	## What to Extract
20	
21	For each agent iteration, review the session and identify:
22	
23	- **New knowledge** — things you learned about the domain, systems, codebase, or environment that are relevant to this agent's role
24	- **Operational lessons** — approaches that worked or didn't work within this agent's scope. If you tried something that failed and then found a better approach, capture that lesson
25	- **Decisions made** — choices and their reasoning, so you don't revisit settled questions
26	- **Recommendations** — practical guidance for future sessions (e.g., "when doing X, use Y approach because Z")
27	- **Role insights** — if the session revealed something about how this agent's role should be understood differently, capture that as a separate role-update topic
28	
29	## How to Write Reflection Topics
30	
31	Write each reflection topic as a plain markdown file in the current agent's `reflections/` directory (the agent whose iteration is running). This directory lives **inside the agent's own directory** — `<lore-agent-repo>/agents/<agent-name>/reflections/`, a sibling of that agent's `lore/`, `lore-context.md`, and `workdir/`. It is **not** at the repo root and **not** at the workspace root. Create it if it does not exist. Writing reflections anywhere else (e.g. `<lore-agent-repo>/reflections/`) is wrong — the merge step, `/lr:check`, and finalization all look only inside the agent directory, so a misplaced reflection is silently lost.
32	
33	Rules:
34	- **One topic per file** — atomic, focused on a single concept or lesson
35	- **Filename**: lowercase kebab-case, descriptive (e.g., `api-retry-behavior.md`, `role-update-scope-change.md`)
36	- **Keep it compact** — only essential information, no filler or general knowledge
37	- **Be specific** — include concrete details, not vague observations
38	- **Include operational guidance** — if the knowledge implies a way of working, state it explicitly
39	
40	Role update topics should be named with a `role-update-` prefix so the merge process can identify them.
41	
42	## What NOT to Extract
43	
44	- Things that are obvious from the code or documentation
45	- General knowledge that anyone could look up
46	- Temporary state that won't matter next session
47	- Verbatim conversation excerpts — distill the insight, don't copy the chat
48	
49	## Example Reflection Topics
50	
51	**`api-timeout-handling.md`**:
52	```
53	The billing API has a 30-second timeout but does not return a timeout
54	error — it returns a generic 500. When retrying billing calls, treat
55	any 500 after 25+ seconds as a likely timeout rather than a server error.
56	Retry with the same idempotency key.
57	```
58	
59	**`role-update-deployment-ownership.md`**:
60	```
61	The team has decided that this agent should also handle deployment
62	verification after releases, not just the release process itself.
63	This includes checking health endpoints and monitoring error rates
64	for 15 minutes post-deploy.
65	```
66	
67	## After Reflection
68	
69	Once you have written all reflection topics for the current agent iteration, inform the user that reflection is complete for that agent and list the topics created. Then, if there are more active agents to reflect, move to the next one. When all active agents have reflected, announce that the full reflection step is complete.
70	
71	The next step — merging reflections into lore — is a separate step triggered by `/lr:merge`. Merge also iterates per active agent in the same host-first order.
72	
````

1	# Merge Process
2	
3	Integrate reflection topics into the agent's existing lore.
4	
5	## Execution model
6	
7	Merge always runs in a subagent, one per active agent, with all subagents launched in parallel. This is uniform for single- and multi-agent sessions — a single-agent session just spawns one subagent. Running merge in a subagent keeps session noise out of merge decisions and gives a clean, focused context.
8	
9	> **Engine note.** The spawn mechanics below describe Claude Code. If your engine profile (`<framework-root>/docs/engines/<engine>.md`, selected at boot) defines a **subagent-spawn override**, follow it instead — e.g. on Codex use `spawn_agent` (role `worker`), not the `Agent` tool, and the **host reads this procedure and passes the steps inline** into each subagent's brief rather than pointing the subagent at this doc. The subagent still boots as its target agent.
10	
11	**Each subagent boots as the agent it is merging for**, using the standard boot procedure. Booting gives the subagent the agent's role, identity, and lore context naturally — the same pattern `/lr:consult` uses. After booting, the subagent runs the process below scoped to its own agent and returns a short summary to the host.
12	
13	Host responsibilities when merge is invoked:
14	
15	1. Collect active agents (host + any attached guests).
16	2. For each, spawn a **`general-purpose`** subagent (merge needs `Write`/`Edit`/`Bash`; `Explore` does not) with a brief such as: _"Boot as agent `<name>` (repo: `<path>`) per `<framework-root>/docs/agent-boot.md`, then run the merge procedure in `<framework-root>/docs/process-merge.md` scoped to yourself. Return a short summary of topics touched, role changes, and any anomalies. Do not commit — finalize handles that."_
17	3. In a multi-agent session, spawn all subagents in parallel (single message with multiple Agent tool calls).
18	4. Collect summaries and report per-agent success/failure to the user. **Retain each subagent's return** — phase 3 (summarize) uses it to compose guest summaries.
19	
20	Each subagent's work is independent: separate `reflections/` directory, separate lore subtree. Merge does not commit — all changes are left uncommitted on disk. Committing is handled once, at the end of `/lr:finalize`, or by the user themselves if merge is invoked standalone.
21	
22	If any subagent fails, the others still proceed.
23	
24	## Inputs (per subagent)
25	
26	- The agent's `lore-context.md` — current compacted knowledge
27	- The agent's `lore/` directory — existing lore topics
28	- The agent's `reflections/` directory — new reflection topics to integrate
29	- The agent's `role.md` — current role description
30	
31	## Process
32	
33	The steps below are what **each subagent** runs once booted as its target agent. The host does not run these steps inline — it orchestrates subagents per the Execution model above and aggregates their summaries.
34	
35	### Step 0: Refresh the Repo
36	
37	Before reading the lore for integration, run the auto-pull procedure in `<framework-root>/docs/auto-pull.md` against the agent's repo. The boot procedure already auto-pulls — this step is defense-in-depth, both because the freshness contract belongs at the merge site explicitly (the moment when stale lore is most damaging) and to cover any boot-pull skip (no remote, network blip) that may have left the repo behind.
38	
39	`--ff-only` is safe even though the merge subagent's working tree is dirty (the `reflections/` from phase 1, or any merge-in-progress edits): git refuses to fast-forward if the operation would clobber uncommitted edits, and otherwise advances `HEAD` cleanly leaving the working tree untouched. See `docs/auto-pull.md` § Invariants.
40	
41	If auto-pull surfaces a non-fast-forward failure, do **not** abort merge — proceed in degraded mode and surface the warning in your return summary so the host can flag it. Concurrent finalize collisions are handled by the push-conflict resolution path in `resolve-conflicts.md`, not here.
42	
43	### Step 1: Read Everything
44	
45	Read all reflection topics, the current `lore-context.md`, and scan the existing `lore/` directory to understand what's already there.
46	
47	### Step 2: Integrate Lore Topics
48	
49	For each reflection topic, decide:
50	
51	1. **Update an existing topic** — if the reflection adds to, refines, or corrects an existing lore topic, update that topic in place. This is the preferred approach.
52	
53	2. **Create a new topic** — if the reflection covers something no existing topic addresses, create a new file in `lore/`. Lowercase kebab-case filename.
54	
55	3. **Remove an obsolete topic** — if a reflection makes an existing topic fundamentally wrong (not just partially outdated), delete it. Git preserves history.
56	
57	When updating or creating topics:
58	- Keep topics under 5000 tokens when possible. Split if too large.
59	- Topics can reference other topics by filename.
60	- Summary topics can link to clusters of related topics.
61	- Include operational recommendations where relevant.
62	- Only essential information — no filler.
63	
64	### Step 3: Handle Role Updates
65	
66	If any reflection topic has a `role-update-` prefix:
67	- Read the current `role.md`
68	- Integrate the role update into the body
69	- Keep `role.md` focused and concise
70	- Preserve the YAML frontmatter. Update the `description` field if the role change warrants a different one-line summary.
71	
72	### Step 4: Update Lore Context
73	
74	Update `lore-context.md` to reflect the new state of the agent's knowledge.
75	
76	**`lore-context.md` is the agent's every-session working knowledge and the entry point to the lore graph — not an index of all topics.** Carry the compacted knowledge needed in essentially every session, plus references to the most important / **summary** topics, and let the graph (summary topics → detail topics) carry the rest. Shape it accordingly:
77	
78	- **Compacted working knowledge** — facts, decisions, and context the agent draws on across most sessions, stated tightly and in the **present tense**.
79	- **Reference summary topics; don't enumerate detail topics** — point at a theme's summary topic and let *it* fan out to the details. Do not list every file in `lore/`. If a significant theme has no summary topic to point at, **create one** (strengthening the graph's mid-tier) rather than inlining the whole cluster here.
80	- **No version-history narrative** — `lore-context.md` is present-tense. Changelog-style "vN did X" annotations and dated step-by-step history belong in git, release notes, or the relevant topic.
81	
82	**Preserve graph navigability.** When you remove something from `lore-context.md`, ensure it still lives in a topic **and** is reachable from a summary topic that `lore-context.md` references — existence somewhere isn't enough; the entry point must still lead there.
83	
84	**Size budget**: keep `lore-context.md` under 50,000 tokens — but shape is the primary discipline, not the ceiling. A well-shaped context sits comfortably under budget; nearing 50K is a drift signal that it has grown toward an index or accumulated history, so **restructure** (lift detail into topics, lean on summary topics, strip narrative) rather than merely summarizing older entries.
85	
86	### Step 5: Cleanup
87	
88	Delete the current agent's `reflections/` directory and all its contents.
89	
90	Merge does not commit — leave all changes uncommitted on disk. In a multi-agent session, return a short summary of what was integrated (topics touched, role changes, any anomalies) to the host. Committing is handled at the end of `/lr:finalize`, or by the user directly if merge is invoked standalone.
91	
92	## Guidelines
93	
94	- **Preserve knowledge** — don't lose information during merging. If removing content from `lore-context.md`, make sure it exists in a lore topic.
95	- **Maintain the graph** — when creating new topics that relate to existing ones, add cross-references.
96	- **Be conservative with deletions** — only delete topics that are fundamentally wrong. Updating is almost always better.
97	- **Respect the agent's voice** — lore should read naturally as the agent's own knowledge, not as third-party documentation.
98	- **Shared topics** — the same topic may legitimately appear in multiple agents' lore if it matters to each of them. Don't try to consolidate into one agent's lore during merge; each agent owns its own copy for its own scope.
99	
```
